The deltas are what arrived on the content channel. Where the daemon opens its reasoning without a `<think>` marker, a later bare `</think>` reveals that prefix as reasoning: the base moves it to `result.thinking`, the deltas it already yielded cannot be recalled, and the join is longer than the settled content for that turn.\r\n\r\nPass `tools` (a non-empty `ToolDefinition[]` from `@orkestrel/tool`) to advertise callable tools for the turn; when the model calls one, `result.tools` is a `ToolCall[]` from that same package (each with a guaranteed `id`, the tool `name`, and parsed `arguments`). Aborting a `stream` mid-flight throws a `ProviderAbortError` whose `partial` holds whatever streamed before the cancel.\r\n\r\n### Surface\r\n\r\nA `Shape` cell holds an interface's data members as bare names in braces, `?` marking an optional member and `plus` introducing its call-signature members, and a type alias's own type literal with a union's arms escaped as `\\|`. An extended interface's name comes before `plus`, with the members it adds after. A function row's `Shape` cell holds its signature, and a guard row's the type it narrows to. A class row's `Shape` cell holds the interface it implements, or its constructor signature where it implements none. A `Shape` cell holds the constant's declared type.\r\n\r\n| API                  | Kind      | Shape                                                                       | Summary                                                                                                                                                                     |\r\n| -------------------- | --------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\r\n| `createOllama`       | function  | `(options: OllamaOptions) => ProviderInterface`                             | Creates a local Ollama inference provider - a `ProviderInterface` over the daemon's `POST /api/chat`, assembling `generate` from the same NDJSON engine as `stream`.        |\r\n| `OllamaProvider`     | class     | `AgentProviderInterface`                                                    | Implements the Ollama `/api/chat` wire over the shared `AgentProvider` engine.                                                                                              |\r\n| `OllamaOptions`      | interface | `ProviderOptions plus { model, url?, keepAlive?, options?, think? }`        | Represents the configuration `createOllama` accepts for the local Ollama backend.                                                                                           |\r\n| `WireChatRequest`    | interface | `{ model, messages, stream, keep_alive, think, options?, tools?, format? }` | Represents the exact `POST /api/chat` request body `OllamaProvider` sends - the internal typed wire contract.                                                               |\r\n| `DEFAULT_OLLAMA_URL` | const     | `string`                                                                    | Names the local Ollama daemon base URL, `'http://localhost:11434'`, assumed when `OllamaOptions.url` is omitted.                                                            |\r\n| `DEFAULT_KEEP_ALIVE` | const     | `string`                                                                    | Names how long the model stays resident after a call - `'5m'` when `OllamaOptions.keepAlive` is omitted, Ollama's own `keep_alive` default, expressed as a duration string. |\r\n| `OLLAMA_CHAT_PATH`   | const     | `string`                                                                    | Names the Ollama chat endpoint appended to the configured base URL.                                                                                                         |\r\n| `mapMessages`        | function  | `(messages: readonly Message[]) => WireChatRequest['messages']`             | Maps conversation turns onto the `/api/chat` wire's minimal message shape.                                                                                                  |\r\n| `extractContent`     | function  | `(record: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>) => string`                     | Extracts the assistant text of one wire record.                                                                                                                             |\r\n| `extractThinking`    | function  | `(record: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>) => string`                     | Extracts the daemon-side reasoning of one wire record.                                                                                                                      |\r\n| `extractUsage`       | function  | `(record: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>) => TokenUsage \\| undefined`    | Extracts the token usage of one wire record.                                                                                                                                |\r\n| `extractTools`       | function  | `(record: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>) => readonly ToolCall[]`        | Extracts the tool calls of one wire record's `message.tool_calls`.                                                                                                          |\r\n| `extractArguments`   | function  | `(value: unknown) => Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>`                     | Extracts a wire `arguments` value as a record.                                                                                                                              |\r\n\r\n`OllamaOptions` extends `ProviderOptions`, so a caller also passes the base's own keys through it: `timeout` (the per-call deadline), `fetch` (the transport), `headers` (the per-request header hook), and `format` (the context-framing default). [`agent.md`](agent.md) states each one.\r\n\r\nThe wire leaves - `mapMessages` and the `extract*` narrowing set - are the pure request projection and response extractions `OllamaProvider` composes. They are exported and unit-tested on their own so the package's highest-risk code, the boundary narrowing of every wire `unknown` through the `@orkestrel/contract` guards, is provable without a daemon. Everything they do not do lives in `@orkestrel/agent`: the deadline, the transport seam, the streaming spine, the splitter, and the assembly of a `ProviderResult`.\r\n\r\n`OllamaProvider` declares `name` (`'ollama'`) and inherits the rest of its data members from the base: `id` is the stable per-instance trace label, and `format` is the context-framing default `OllamaOptions.format` supplies (see [Context framing](#context-framing)). Its wire seams are documented under [Methods](#methods), and the `generate` / `stream` boundary it inherits under `AgentProviderInterface` in [`agent.md`](agent.md). `AgentProvider` / `AgentProviderInterface` / `ProviderInterface` / `ProviderOptions` / `ProviderRequest` / `ProviderIncrement` / `ProviderParserInterface` / `ProviderResult` / `ProviderDelta` / `ProviderStreamOptions` / `ProviderError` / `isProviderError` / `ProviderAbortError` / `isProviderAbortError` / `Message` / `ContextFormat` are owned by `@orkestrel/agent`; `ToolDefinition` / `ToolCall` by `@orkestrel/tool`; the `TokenUsage` shape by `@orkestrel/budget`; `createNDJSONParser` by `@orkestrel/ndjson`. All are reused here, never redefined.\r\n\r\n## Methods\r\n\r\nThe wire seams `OllamaProvider` declares - exactly the members of `AgentProviderInterface` that the base leaves open. The runtime never calls them directly: a caller drives `generate` or `stream`, and the engine calls these to frame, project, decode, and drain the Ollama wire. Those inherited calls, the `id` / `name` / `format` data members, and the `ProviderParserInterface` the seam returns are documented in [`agent.md`](agent.md).\r\n\r\n#### `OllamaProvider`\r\n\r\nThe `frame` method opens the call's framing state and the `finish` method drains it; the `body` method writes the request and the `read` method reads one record back.\r\n\r\n| Method   | Returns                                            | Summary                                                                        |\r\n| -------- | -------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |\r\n| `frame`  | `ProviderParserInterface`                          | Creates fresh NDJSON framing state for a call.                                 |\r\n| `body`   | `WireChatRequest`                                  | Projects conversation turns and per-call options onto the Ollama request body. |\r\n| `read`   | `ProviderIncrement`                                | Extracts a record's content, reasoning, tools, and completed usage report.     |\r\n| `finish` | `ReadonlyArray<Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>>` | Recovers a final NDJSON record that arrived without its line terminator.       |\r\n\r\n## Contract\r\n\r\nThese invariants hold across `src/core` \u001d `ollama.md`. The engine contract itself - `ProviderInterface`, `AgentProvider`, `ProviderResult`, `ProviderError`, `ProviderAbortError` - is in `@orkestrel/agent`, and [`agent.md`](agent.md) states it.\r\n\r\n1. **Doc \u001d source bijection.** Every `function` / `class` / `const` / `interface` / `type` row in the `## Surface` table is a real export of the `src/core` surface, and every export appears as a Surface row - exhaustive, both directions.\r\n2. **Imports each boundary from its owner, no cycle.** `OllamaProvider` extends `AgentProvider` and implements `AgentProviderInterface`; that base, `ProviderRequest`, `ProviderIncrement`, `ProviderParserInterface`, `ProviderInterface`, `ProviderOptions`, and `Message` come from `@orkestrel/agent`. `createNDJSONParser` comes from `@orkestrel/ndjson`, `ToolCall` from `@orkestrel/tool`, `TokenUsage` from `@orkestrel/budget`, and the guards (`isRecord` / `isString` / `isNumber` / `parseJSONAs`) from `@orkestrel/contract`. The provider errors are owned by `@orkestrel/agent` and `ToolDefinition` by `@orkestrel/tool`: `src/core` imports neither, and both reach a caller through the base - the base throws the errors this wire's failures become, and it accepts the `ToolDefinition[]` a call advertises. Each dependency is one-way and never imports from `@src/core`. No module under `src/` imports `@orkestrel/timeout` - the base arms the deadline - and the manifest declares it as a development dependency for the guide's bounding pattern, which imports it as a consumer would.\r\n3. **The `/api/chat` wire body.** `OllamaProvider` POSTs to `${url}${OLLAMA_CHAT_PATH}`, defaulting `url` to `DEFAULT_OLLAMA_URL` and `keep_alive` to `DEFAULT_KEEP_ALIVE`. The body carries `model`, the mapped `messages`, `stream: true` on every call, `keep_alive`, and `think` (the per-call `ProviderStreamOptions.think` override when present, else `OllamaOptions.think`, default `false`), adding `options` only when configured, `format` only when the call supplies a `ProviderStreamOptions.schema`, and `tools` only when a non-empty `ToolDefinition[]` is passed. Each tool maps to `{ type: 'function', function: { name } }`, with `description` and `parameters` added only when the corresponding `ToolDefinition` fields are supplied. Messages map to the wire's minimal `{ role, content }` turn, with `tool_calls` added only on a turn that replays them and `images` only on a multimodal turn. A non-OK status is the base's failure, not this package's: it throws `ProviderError` with code `'HTTP'`, the response `status`, and a message bounded to an excerpt of the response body.\r\n4. **Configurable `think` on the wire; the base's splitter keeps the assembled content clean.** For a thinking-capable model (for example `qwen3`) the per-request `think` flag is the only wire-level reasoning control (its native renderer honours neither the qwen3 `/no_think` token nor a Modelfile `PARAMETER think false`). It is configurable through `OllamaOptions.think` (default `false` - so the general-purpose provider stays immediate for non-thinking models and tests fast) and overrideable per call through `ProviderStreamOptions.think`. Set `think: true` when the caller displays reasoning separately from the answer: the daemon then separates reasoning natively, returning it on the distinct `message.thinking` channel, which `read` reports and the engine yields as `ProviderDelta` `{ channel: 'thinking', text }` and accumulates onto `ProviderResult.thinking`. Either way the base's `split` behaviour stays armed, because a daemon may ignore `think: false` for a thinking model and render reasoning inline as `<think>.</think>` content; `OllamaProvider` passes no `split` to `super`, so the default separation applies and the assembled `content` is the splitter's clean accumulation. The reasoning never re-enters the conversation.\r\n5. **One NDJSON path.** Every call consumes NDJSON - one JSON object per `\\n`-terminated line. `frame()` returns `createNDJSONParser()`, fresh per call, so no call inherits another's half-read record, and the base pairs it with a streaming `TextDecoder` so a record split across byte reads is reassembled. At end of input the base calls `finish(parser)`, which feeds a trailing `\\n` through the parser: a non-conformant proxy's final unterminated line is recovered rather than silently dropped. `OllamaProvider` passes no `strict` to `super`, so a stream that ends without a settled record still assembles its result from the deltas it carried.\r\n6. **`stream` yields deltas and returns the assembled result.** The engine yields each non-empty clean content delta as `{ channel: 'content', text }` and each daemon-side reasoning delta as `{ channel: 'thinking', text }`; its return value is the assembled `ProviderResult` whose `content` is the splitter's authoritative clean accumulation - the concatenation of the yielded content deltas, except across an implicit-open reclassification, where the reasoning prefix had already streamed before a bare `</think>` revealed it and the yields cannot be recalled - plus any tool calls collected across lines, the usage from the `done` line, and the separated `thinking` when the turn produced any.\r\n7. **Usage from the `done` line (reuses `TokenUsage`).** `read` reports `usage` only for a record whose `done` is `true` and whose `prompt_eval_count` and `eval_count` are both numbers - mapped to `{ prompt, completion, total: prompt + completion }`, the `TokenUsage` shape, imported rather than redefined. A delta line carries no counts and a `done: false` line's counts are ignored, so neither contributes usage, and `ProviderResult.usage` is absent when the turn reported none.\r\n8. **Tool-call extraction with id-generation (no `as`).** `result.tools` is the model's `message.tool_calls`, each entry narrowed to `{ id, name, arguments }`: the entry and its `function` must be records and `name` a string (else the entry is dropped); `arguments` is the wire object as-is, a JSON string parsed to a record, or `{}` when neither; `id` is the wire's `id` when a string, else a freshly minted `crypto.randomUUID()`. An empty result `tools` is never surfaced - its absence means \"no calls\".\r\n9. **Boundary narrowing - all wire `unknown` through guards, never `as`.** Every value read off the wire (each NDJSON record, `message`, `content`, `thinking`, the usage counts, `tool_calls`, `arguments`) arrives as `unknown` and is narrowed through the `@orkestrel/contract` guards - never a type assertion. A missing or malformed field degrades to a sensible default (empty content, no usage, `{}` arguments), never a throw - the one external boundary kept honest.\r\n10. **The base owns everything that is not the wire.** The per-call deadline (`OllamaOptions.timeout`, `120_000`ms when omitted, folded with the caller's signal so either cancels the request and the timer is always cleared), the cancellation rule (a `stream` cancelled mid-flight throws `ProviderAbortError` carrying the partial, and the reader and parser are released in a `finally`), the transport seam (`OllamaOptions.fetch` and the per-request `OllamaOptions.headers` hook, awaited inside the deadline and raced against the combined signal), and the bounded error read all live in `AgentProvider`. `OllamaOptions` extends `ProviderOptions`, so those keys are the base's and behave identically for every provider built on it; [`agent.md`](agent.md) states each rule once.\r\n11. **Context-framing `format` (provider-default cascade level, expose-only).** `OllamaOptions.format` is an optional `ContextFormat` (from `@orkestrel/agent`) - the provider's context-framing default, passed to `super` and exposed as `provider.format`. It is the provider-default level of the `AgentContext` build cascade (beats the managers' built-in framing, beaten by a manager-options or per-item override; see [`agent.md`](agent.md)), read by the Agent when it assembles the prompt. Omitted ? `undefined` (framing-agnostic; core's built-in framing applies unchanged). It is **never sent on the `/api/chat` wire**: it is consumed by core, absent from the request `body`, and unrelated to Ollama's structured-output `format` parameter - that one is sent in the request `body`, but only when a per-call `ProviderStreamOptions.schema` is supplied - the framing default and that wire parameter only share a word.\r\n12. **Event-free.** A pure functional boundary - no Emitter, no events. Each call is a function of its arguments.\r\n13. **A core face that runs in a browser.** The package publishes the `.` entry alone, built from `src/core`, which imports no `node:*` module and no DOM global; the scoped core typecheck compiles it with the `ESNext` and `WebWorker` libraries and no ambient `@types`, so neither Node's globals nor the DOM's are in scope, and the base binds `globalThis.fetch` to its own receiver so a browser does not throw `Illegal invocation` on a bare transport reference. On 2026-09-14 in Chrome 148 the built `@orkestrel/ollama` core entry and its whole `@orkestrel` import closure loaded as ES modules with no console error: `OllamaProvider` drove the local daemon directly for a settled answer carrying its usage counts, a cancel mid-stream returned `ProviderAbortError` holding the partial that had streamed, and the same page reached that daemon through a `createRelay` server, where a wrong bearer arrived as `ProviderError` with code `'HTTP'` and status `401`. The daemon is the remaining limit - a page reaches `/api/chat` directly only where the daemon, or a proxy in front of it, answers the page's origin; otherwise relay the call through your own server ([Relaying through your own server](#relaying-through-your-own-server)).\r\n14. **Tested live against a real local Ollama (no `skipIf`).** The live tests run against a real Ollama daemon - no mocks, only genuine third-party calls - model `qwen3.5:2b-q4_K_M`, with `OLLAMA_HOST` / `OLLAMA_MODEL` overridable. Unlike the other surfaces, the dedicated `service` project requires the daemon: `tests/setupService.ts` throws a clear error if it is unreachable and warms the model (a `num_predict: 1` chat) before the suite, so the live tests run unconditionally (no `describe.skipIf`). The project runs serially (`fileParallelism: false`) with a 120s test/hook timeout so a cold load cannot flake it; `keep_alive` keeps the model resident across files, and `bash scripts/ollama.sh` brings the daemon and model up before the battery in CI. Assertions are structural - they hold whatever wording a small model produces - and never pin exact output. The hermetic half sits in `tests/src/core/`, and the relay round trip is proved hermetically in [`tests/src/core/integration.test.ts`](../tests/src/core/integration.test.ts) and live in [`tests/service/relay.test.ts`](../tests/service/relay.test.ts).\r\n15. **Doc \u001d source method bijection.** The `## Methods` table lists exactly the members `OllamaProvider` declares - `frame`, `body`, `read`, and `finish` - and the class declares exactly those. `generate` and `stream` reach a caller through the same class but are declared once, in `AgentProvider`, and documented in [`agent.md`](agent.md).\r\n\r\n## Patterns\r\n\r\n### `createOllama` + `generate`\r\n\r\nThe dominant single-shot use: one prompt, one assembled result.\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nimport { createAbort } from '@orkestrel/abort'\r\nimport { createOllama } from '@orkestrel/ollama'\r\n\r\nconst provider = createOllama({ model: 'qwen3.5:2b-q4_K_M', options: { temperature: 0 } })\r\nconst abort = createAbort()\r\nconst messages = [\r\n\t{ id: '1', role: 'user', content: 'Summarize the release notes for version 2.0.' },\r\n] as const\r\n\r\nconst result = await provider.generate(messages, abort.signal)\r\nconsole.log(result.content)\r\nif (result.usage) charge(result.usage) // fold into a token budget\r\n```\r\n\r\n### Bounding a call with a budget + timeout\r\n\r\n`generate` / `stream` take a plain `AbortSignal`, so fold an abort, a timeout, and a token budget into one bound through `AbortSignal.any` - whichever trips first cancels the call. This caller-side bound stacks on top of the base's own armed deadline (`OllamaOptions.timeout`, `120_000`ms when omitted): the request dies when either fires, so you get an external cancel or budget and a hard per-call ceiling together.\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nimport { createAbort } from '@orkestrel/abort'\r\nimport { createTimeout } from '@orkestrel/timeout'\r\nimport { createTokenBudget } from '@orkestrel/budget'\r\nimport { createOllama } from '@orkestrel/ollama'\r\n\r\nconst provider = createOllama({ model: 'qwen3.5:2b-q4_K_M' })\r\nconst messages = [{ id: '1', role: 'user', content: 'Say hello.' }] as const\r\nconst abort = createAbort() // external cancel\r\nconst timeout = createTimeout({ ms: 30_000 }) // wall-clock deadline\r\nconst budget = createTokenBudget({ max: 50_000, scope: 'total' }) // cost ceiling\r\ntimeout.start()\r\nbudget.start()\r\n\r\nconst bound = AbortSignal.any([abort.signal, timeout.signal, budget.signal])\r\nconst result = await provider.generate(messages, bound)\r\nbudget.consume(result.usage ?? { prompt: 0, completion: 0, total: 0 })\r\n```\r\n\r\n### Projecting the wire without a daemon\r\n\r\nThe seam members are ordinary methods, so a test or a debugging session can read the exact request this provider would send and the exact contribution one daemon record makes - with no socket, no daemon, and no model. Construct `OllamaProvider` directly: `createOllama` returns the narrower `ProviderInterface`, which exposes the call boundary alone.\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nimport { OllamaProvider } from '@orkestrel/ollama'\r\n\r\nconst provider = new OllamaProvider({ model: 'qwen3.5:2b-q4_K_M', keepAlive: '9m' })\r\nconst request = provider.body({\r\n\tmessages: [{ id: '1', role: 'user', content: 'Say hello.' }],\r\n\toptions: { think: true },\r\n})\r\nrequest.stream // true - every call streams\r\nrequest.think // true - the per-call override beats the constructed default\r\nrequest.keep_alive // '9m'\r\nrequest.messages // [{ role: 'user', content: 'Say hello.' }] - ids stay local\r\n\r\nconst parser = provider.frame()\r\nparser.parse('{\"message\":{\"content\":\"Hel\"}}\\n') // [{ message: { content: 'Hel' } }]\r\nprovider.read({ message: { content: 'lo' }, done: true, prompt_eval_count: 3, eval_count: 4 })\r\n// { content: 'lo', thinking: '', tools: [], usage: { prompt: 3, completion: 4, total: 7 } }\r\nprovider.finish(parser) // [] - the parser held nothing back\r\n```\r\n\r\n### Running in the browser\r\n\r\nThe published face is core, so the same `createOllama` call runs unchanged in a browser module. Point `url` at whatever the page can actually reach: the daemon's own origin where it answers that origin, or your own server standing in front of it. A page that reaches a daemon directly is a development arrangement - it puts the model endpoint on the network the page runs on - so prefer one of the server patterns that follow for anything a user runs.\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nimport { createOllama } from '@orkestrel/ollama'\r\n\r\nconst provider = createOllama({\r\n\tmodel: 'qwen3.5:2b-q4_K_M',\r\n\turl: 'https://llm.example.com', // the daemon's origin, or your proxy in front of it\r\n})\r\nprovider.name // 'ollama'\r\n```\r\n\r\n### Relaying through your own server\r\n\r\nThe agent-level relay keeps the whole provider on your server and gives the browser a `ProviderInterface` over your own route. The browser sends identified messages, tools, and per-call options; the server answers with NDJSON frames carrying the deltas and the settled result, so `thinking`, `tools`, and `usage` survive the hop and a server-side cancel arrives as a `ProviderAbortError` with its partial. Nothing about the model, the vendor, or the daemon's address reaches the page - which is what separates this from the transparent wire proxy that follows.\r\n\r\nEach half that follows runs in its own process: the server half goes in your server, the browser half in your browser bundle.\r\n\r\nOn the server, mount `createRelay` over a real `OllamaProvider` on any fetch-standard router, and serve that router. This composition routes with an `@orkestrel/router` dispatcher and serves it with an `@orkestrel/server` listener: `start` binds the configured host and port and resolves the port it bound, and `stop` drains the turns still in flight and then closes. `authorize` is mandatory, and minting and validating the session credential stays your application's job.\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nimport { createRelay } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\nimport { createOllama } from '@orkestrel/ollama'\r\nimport { createDispatcher } from '@orkestrel/router'\r\nimport { createServer } from '@orkestrel/server'\r\n\r\nconst handler = createRelay({\r\n\tprovider: createOllama({ model: 'qwen3.5:2b-q4_K_M' }),\r\n\tauthorize: (request) => request.headers.get('authorization') === 'Bearer session-token',\r\n})\r\nconst dispatcher = createDispatcher({\r\n\troutes: [{ method: 'POST', path: '/inference', handler }],\r\n})\r\nconst server = createServer({ dispatcher, state: () => ({}), host: '127.0.0.1', port: 8787 })\r\nconst port = await server.start() // the port it bound - an ephemeral one when `port` is omitted\r\nconst url = `http://127.0.0.1:${port}/inference` // the route the browser half dials\r\n// await server.stop() at shutdown - it drains the turns still in flight, then closes\r\n```\r\n\r\n`createRelay` returns a plain `(request: Request) => Promise<Response>` handler and stops there, so a runtime adapter turns the runtime's inbound request into a `Request`, hands the dispatcher's `Response` back to the runtime, and aborts that request's signal when the client disconnects, so a reader that goes away cancels the daemon turn instead of leaving it running. `@orkestrel/server` supplies that adapter ([`server.md`](server.md)), and a runtime whose own handler already takes a `Request` and returns a `Response` needs none.\r\n\r\nIn the browser, `createRelayProvider` drives that route like a local provider over the page's own `fetch`. `@orkestrel/agent` declares no parser dependency, so the browser application supplies one - `createNDJSONParser` from `@orkestrel/ndjson` here.\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nimport { createAbort } from '@orkestrel/abort'\r\nimport { createRelayProvider } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\nimport { createNDJSONParser } from '@orkestrel/ndjson'\r\n\r\ndeclare const url: string // the route the server half bound\r\nconst abort = createAbort()\r\nconst messages = [{ id: '1', role: 'user', content: 'Say hello.' }] as const\r\n\r\nconst browser = createRelayProvider({\r\n\turl,\r\n\tparser: createNDJSONParser,\r\n\theaders: () => ({ authorization: 'Bearer session-token' }),\r\n})\r\nconst relayed = await browser.generate(messages, abort.signal)\r\nrelayed.content // the daemon's answer, reassembled from the relay's frames\r\n```\r\n\r\nA refusal never becomes a frame: a wrong credential reaches the browser as a `ProviderError` with code `'HTTP'` and status `401`, and the provider on the server is never entered. [`agent.md`](agent.md) states the frame vocabulary, the byte limit, and the rest of the refusal statuses.\r\n\r\n### Routing through your own server (obfuscated tokens)\r\n\r\nA transparent wire proxy is the lighter shape: leave the provider in the browser, point `url` at a thin server of your own that forwards `/api/chat` to the daemon, and use `headers` to attach a generated token that server validates. The client never handles the real key - the key lives only on that server, and the `headers` hook supplies whatever short-lived token it expects. The custom `fetch` swaps the transport (a browser fetch, an instrumented wrapper) without touching the wire protocol. Omitting the transport and the header hook leaves the bound global `fetch` and only `Content-Type: application/json`.\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nimport { createAbort } from '@orkestrel/abort'\r\nimport { createOllama } from '@orkestrel/ollama'\r\n\r\ndeclare function mintToken(): Promise<string> // your short-lived token, minted per request\r\n\r\nconst provider = createOllama({\r\n\tmodel: 'qwen3.5:2b-q4_K_M',\r\n\turl: 'https://my-app.example.com/llm', // your server, which forwards to the real LLM\r\n\theaders: async () => ({ authorization: `Bearer ${await mintToken()}` }), // server validates this\r\n\t// fetch: myFetch, // optional: inject a custom transport\r\n})\r\nconst abort = createAbort()\r\nconst messages = [{ id: '1', role: 'user', content: 'Say hello.' }] as const\r\nconst result = await provider.generate(messages, abort.signal)\r\n```\r\n\r\nThe `headers` hook is called once per request (async, so a token can be refreshed each call); its result merges on top of the base `Content-Type`, so it adds an authorization header without disturbing the JSON body. What this shape cannot do is hide the vendor: the page still speaks Ollama's `/api/chat`, still names the model, and still needs a proxy that speaks that same wire, so switching vendors means shipping a different provider to the browser. Relay instead when the page must stay ignorant of what answers it.\r\n\r\n### Context framing\r\n\r\n`OllamaOptions.format` is the provider's context-framing default - a `ContextFormat` (from `@orkestrel/agent`) declaring how this provider's models prefer the context sections framed (for example XML group wrappers vs. Markdown headers). It is the **provider-default level** of the `AgentContext` build cascade (see [`agent.md`](agent.md)): it beats the managers' built-in framing but is beaten by a manager-options or per-item override. `createOllama` accepts it directly and `OllamaProvider` hands it to the base, which exposes it as `provider.format`; the Agent reads it when it assembles the prompt. Omit it and the provider is framing-agnostic - core's built-in section framing applies unchanged.\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nimport { createOllama } from '@orkestrel/ollama'\r\n\r\nconst provider = createOllama({\r\n\tmodel: 'qwen3.5:2b-q4_K_M',\r\n\tformat: {\r\n\t\tinstructions: {\r\n\t\t\topen: '<instructions>',\r\n\t\t\trender: (instruction) => `<instruction>${instruction.content}</instruction>`,\r\n\t\t\tclose: '</instructions>',\r\n\t\t},\r\n\t},\r\n})\r\nprovider.format // the ContextFormat preceding - read by AgentContext.build()\r\n```\r\n\r\n**Not the wire `format`.** This is the prompt-context framing consumed by `AgentContext.build()` - it is **expose-only** and is never sent on the `/api/chat` request. It is unrelated to Ollama's `/api/chat` `format` parameter (structured output / JSON schema): that wire field is driven by a different, per-call source - `ProviderStreamOptions.schema` (from `@orkestrel/agent`) - forwarded verbatim as `format` and omitted from the body when `schema` is undefined. The framing default and the wire parameter share a word and nothing else: the framing default is a hint the daemon never sees, and the wire parameter is a per-call constraint the provider never exposes back.\r\n\r\n### Narrowing a failed call with `isProviderError`\r\n\r\nBranch on the machine-readable `code` and the HTTP `status` rather than parsing the thrown message. The engine throws one error class for every provider built on it, so the same `catch` serves a local daemon, a proxy, and a relay.\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nimport { createAbort } from '@orkestrel/abort'\r\nimport { isProviderError } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\nimport { createOllama } from '@orkestrel/ollama'\r\n\r\nconst provider = createOllama({ model: 'qwen3.5:2b-q4_K_M' })\r\nconst abort = createAbort()\r\nconst messages = [{ id: '1', role: 'user', content: 'Reply with exactly: ok' }] as const\r\n\r\ntry {\r\n\tawait provider.generate(messages, abort.signal)\r\n} catch (error) {\r\n\tif (isProviderError(error) && error.code === 'HTTP' && error.status === 404) {\r\n\t\t// the configured model isn't pulled\r\n\t} else if (isProviderError(error) && error.code === 'HTTP' && error.status === 400) {\r\n\t\t// malformed request (for example an empty model string)\r\n\t} else {\r\n\t\tthrow error\r\n\t}\r\n}\r\n```\r\n\r\nA cancel is outside that taxonomy: the caller's signal and the deadline both throw `ProviderAbortError`, which `isProviderAbortError` narrows, and its `partial` carries what had streamed.\r\n\r\n### Practices\r\n\r\n- **Import the engine from `@orkestrel/agent`, the wire from `@orkestrel/ollama`** - `ProviderInterface` / `ProviderResult` / `ProviderError` / `isProviderError` / `isProviderAbortError` live in `@orkestrel/agent`; `createOllama` / `OllamaProvider` / `OllamaOptions` live in `@orkestrel/ollama`.\r\n- **Import tool-call shapes from `@orkestrel/tool`** - `ToolDefinition` / `ToolCall` live with the tool runtime, while the provider contract that consumes them stays in `@orkestrel/agent`.\r\n- **Bound every call** - pass an `AbortSignal` (an abort, or an `AbortSignal.any` over abort + timeout + budget) so a request can be cancelled, deadlined, or capped.\r\n- **Recover the stream's partial** - wrap a driven `stream` in `try`/`catch` and narrow with `isProviderAbortError` to keep the content that arrived before a cancel.\r\n- **Branch on the code and the status, not the message** - narrow a caught value with `isProviderError` and read `error.code` and `error.status` (`404` for an unpulled model) instead of parsing `error.message`.\r\n- **Fold usage into a budget** - `result.usage` is the `TokenUsage` shape; `consume` it per turn to enforce a token ceiling.\r\n- **Give a thinking model headroom** - with `think: true`, a reasoning model (for example `qwen3.5:2b` at `temperature: 0`) can spend 200+ tokens on `message.thinking` before any `content` token arrives; a tight `num_predict` cap or a tight external token budget can exhaust before content starts, yielding empty `result.content` - and `@orkestrel/agent`'s budget enforcement can abort mid-stream with `partial: true` if the cap is hit while thinking is still draining it. Size `num_predict` and the budget with thinking overhead in mind for any model run with `think: true`.\r\n- **Tune through `options`** - pass sampling parameters (`temperature`, `seed`, and `num_predict`) through `OllamaOptions.options`; they forward verbatim to the wire.\r\n- **Observe at the call site** - the provider publishes no events; read `stream`'s deltas and its returned result for everything a turn produced.\r\n\r\n## Tests\r\n\r\nThe hermetic projects - `src:core`, `setup`, `guides`, `conformance`, `policy`, and `config` - run with no daemon and no network. {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_17","type":"command_execution","command":"\"C:\\\\WINDOWS\\\\System32\\\\WindowsPowerShell\\\\v1.0\\\\powershell.exe\" -Command '$n=0; Get-Content -Encoding utf8 guides/ollama.md | ForEach-Object { $n++; if ($n -le 200) { '\"'{0}: {1}' -f \"'$n,$_ } }; Get-Content -Encoding utf8 ../scaffold/.orkestrel/campaign/o6-gates.log.txt'","aggregated_output":"1: # Ollama\r\n2: \r\n3: > A typed local-LLM provider for the `@orkestrel` line: the Ollama daemon's `POST /api/chat`\r\n4: > wire carried on the shared `AgentProvider` engine from `@orkestrel/agent`, with NDJSON\r\n5: > streaming, tool calls, thinking, and usage accounting narrowed off the wire through\r\n6: > `@orkestrel/contract` guards and no Ollama SDK dependency.\r\n7: \r\n8: This provider lets an Agent run against a real model on `localhost` - one small local model, no cloud dependency, and no API key. It supplies the Ollama wire and nothing else. `AgentProvider`, the HTTP engine in `@orkestrel/agent`, owns the deadline, the transport, the `headers` hook, the request, the bounded error read, the chunk decoder, the reasoning separation, and the result assembly. `OllamaProvider` extends that engine and fills the wire seams: the `frame` seam returns a fresh NDJSON parser, the `body` seam projects a `ProviderRequest` onto the `/api/chat` request, the `read` seam decodes one record into a `ProviderIncrement`, and the `finish` seam recovers a final line the daemon left unterminated. What a caller drives - `generate`, `stream`, `id`, `format` - is the base's, and [`agent.md`](agent.md) documents it.\r\n9: \r\n10: The design is deliberately spare - an external boundary kept honest. Every `unknown` wire value is narrowed through the `@orkestrel/contract` guards (`isRecord`, `isString`, `isNumber`) rather than a type assertion, and a missing or malformed field degrades to a sensible default (empty content, no usage, `{}` arguments) rather than a throw. Every call streams: the request always carries `stream: true`, and `generate` drains the same NDJSON path `stream` exposes, so neither call can report content the other would not. The wire `think` flag is configurable through `OllamaOptions.think` (default `false`) and overrideable per call through `ProviderStreamOptions.think`; with `think: true` the daemon returns reasoning on the separate `message.thinking` channel, streamed live as `thinking` deltas. Either way the base's splitter separates any `<think>` span the daemon inlines anyway, so the assembled `content` is clean and the reasoning surfaces as `ProviderResult.thinking`, never in the conversation. A per-call `ProviderStreamOptions.schema` (a JSON schema object, from `@orkestrel/agent`) forwards verbatim as the wire's structured-output `format` field, and is omitted from the request when no schema is supplied. Token usage reuses the `TokenUsage` shape rather than minting its own.\r\n11: \r\n12: The dependency is strictly one-way: this surface imports the base, the contract types, and the error from `@orkestrel/agent`, the parser factory from `@orkestrel/ndjson`, tool-call shapes from `@orkestrel/tool`, the usage shape from `@orkestrel/budget`, and the guards from `@orkestrel/contract` - those packages never import from here. The published face is core, so `src/core` reaches no `node:*` module and no DOM global and the same build serves a server process and a browser page. It is tested live against `qwen3.5:2b-q4_K_M` in a dedicated `service` test project that requires the daemon and warms the model first, with no `skipIf`, while the `src:core` project stays hermetic on canned-transport and recording-proxy assertions that pass with the daemon down. Source: [`src/core`](../src/core). Surfaced through the `@orkestrel/ollama` barrel, aliased `@src/core` inside this repo.\r\n13: \r\n14: ## Surface\r\n15: \r\n16: The 80% case: create a provider once, then `generate` a turn against a conversation, bounding the call with an `AbortSignal` (from `@orkestrel/abort`). Messages are the abstract `Message` shape from `@orkestrel/agent` (`{ id, role, content }`), so the same conversation drives any provider.\r\n17: \r\n18: ```ts\r\n19: import { createAbort } from '@orkestrel/abort'\r\n20: import { createOllama } from '@orkestrel/ollama'\r\n21: \r\n22: const provider = createOllama({ model: 'qwen3.5:2b-q4_K_M' })\r\n23: const abort = createAbort()\r\n24: const messages = [{ id: '1', role: 'user', content: 'Reply with exactly: ok' }] as const\r\n25: \r\n26: const result = await provider.generate(messages, abort.signal)\r\n27: result.content // the assistant's answer text\r\n28: result.usage // { prompt, completion, total } when the wire reported counts - folds into a token budget\r\n29: ```\r\n30: \r\n31: `generate` resolves the assembled `ProviderResult` (content + any tool calls + any usage). For live output, `stream` is the same call streamed: it yields `ProviderDelta`s (`content` for answer text, `thinking` for live reasoning) and returns the assembled result when the stream completes. Drive the generator and read its terminal `value`:\r\n32: \r\n33: ```ts\r\n34: import { createAbort } from '@orkestrel/abort'\r\n35: import { isProviderAbortError } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n36: import { createOllama } from '@orkestrel/ollama'\r\n37: \r\n38: const provider = createOllama({ model: 'qwen3.5:2b-q4_K_M' })\r\n39: const abort = createAbort()\r\n40: const messages = [{ id: '1', role: 'user', content: 'Say hello.' }] as const\r\n41: const answer: string[] = []\r\n42: const reasoning: string[] = []\r\n43: \r\n44: try {\r\n45: \tconst generator = provider.stream(messages, abort.signal)\r\n46: \tlet step = await generator.next()\r\n47: \twhile (!step.done) {\r\n48: \t\tif (step.value.channel === 'content') answer.push(step.value.text)\r\n49: \t\tif (step.value.channel === 'thinking') reasoning.push(step.value.text)\r\n50: \t\tstep = await generator.next()\r\n51: \t}\r\n52: \tconst result = step.value // the assembled ProviderResult\r\n53: \tresult.content // the answer - the settled content is the authoritative one\r\n54: \tanswer.join('') // what arrived on the content channel, which a reclassified <think> span leaves longer\r\n55: } catch (error) {\r\n56: \tif (isProviderAbortError(error))\r\n57: \t\tanswer.push(error.partial.content) // recover what streamed\r\n58: \telse throw error\r\n59: }\r\n60: ```\r\n61: \r\n62: Read the answer from the settled `result.content`, never from the joined deltas. The deltas are what arrived on the content channel. Where the daemon opens its reasoning without a `<think>` marker, a later bare `</think>` reveals that prefix as reasoning: the base moves it to `result.thinking`, the deltas it already yielded cannot be recalled, and the join is longer than the settled content for that turn.\r\n63: \r\n64: Pass `tools` (a non-empty `ToolDefinition[]` from `@orkestrel/tool`) to advertise callable tools for the turn; when the model calls one, `result.tools` is a `ToolCall[]` from that same package (each with a guaranteed `id`, the tool `name`, and parsed `arguments`). Aborting a `stream` mid-flight throws a `ProviderAbortError` whose `partial` holds whatever streamed before the cancel.\r\n65: \r\n66: ### Surface\r\n67: \r\n68: A `Shape` cell holds an interface's data members as bare names in braces, `?` marking an optional member and `plus` introducing its call-signature members, and a type alias's own type literal with a union's arms escaped as `\\|`. An extended interface's name comes before `plus`, with the members it adds after. A function row's `Shape` cell holds its signature, and a guard row's the type it narrows to. A class row's `Shape` cell holds the interface it implements, or its constructor signature where it implements none. A `Shape` cell holds the constant's declared type.\r\n69: \r\n70: | API                  | Kind      | Shape                                                                       | Summary                                                                                                                                                                     |\r\n71: | -------------------- | --------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\r\n72: | `createOllama`       | function  | `(options: OllamaOptions) => ProviderInterface`                             | Creates a local Ollama inference provider - a `ProviderInterface` over the daemon's `POST /api/chat`, assembling `generate` from the same NDJSON engine as `stream`.        |\r\n73: | `OllamaProvider`     | class     | `AgentProviderInterface`                                                    | Implements the Ollama `/api/chat` wire over the shared `AgentProvider` engine.                                                                                              |\r\n74: | `OllamaOptions`      | interface | `ProviderOptions plus { model, url?, keepAlive?, options?, think? }`        | Represents the configuration `createOllama` accepts for the local Ollama backend.                                                                                           |\r\n75: | `WireChatRequest`    | interface | `{ model, messages, stream, keep_alive, think, options?, tools?, format? }` | Represents the exact `POST /api/chat` request body `OllamaProvider` sends - the internal typed wire contract.                                                               |\r\n76: | `DEFAULT_OLLAMA_URL` | const     | `string`                                                                    | Names the local Ollama daemon base URL, `'http://localhost:11434'`, assumed when `OllamaOptions.url` is omitted.                                                            |\r\n77: | `DEFAULT_KEEP_ALIVE` | const     | `string`                                                                    | Names how long the model stays resident after a call - `'5m'` when `OllamaOptions.keepAlive` is omitted, Ollama's own `keep_alive` default, expressed as a duration string. |\r\n78: | `OLLAMA_CHAT_PATH`   | const     | `string`                                                                    | Names the Ollama chat endpoint appended to the configured base URL.                                                                                                         |\r\n79: | `mapMessages`        | function  | `(messages: readonly Message[]) => WireChatRequest['messages']`             | Maps conversation turns onto the `/api/chat` wire's minimal message shape.                                                                                                  |\r\n80: | `extractContent`     | function  | `(record: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>) => string`                     | Extracts the assistant text of one wire record.                                                                                                                             |\r\n81: | `extractThinking`    | function  | `(record: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>) => string`                     | Extracts the daemon-side reasoning of one wire record.                                                                                                                      |\r\n82: | `extractUsage`       | function  | `(record: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>) => TokenUsage \\| undefined`    | Extracts the token usage of one wire record.                                                                                                                                |\r\n83: | `extractTools`       | function  | `(record: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>) => readonly ToolCall[]`        | Extracts the tool calls of one wire record's `message.tool_calls`.                                                                                                          |\r\n84: | `extractArguments`   | function  | `(value: unknown) => Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>`                     | Extracts a wire `arguments` value as a record.                                                                                                                              |\r\n85: \r\n86: `OllamaOptions` extends `ProviderOptions`, so a caller also passes the base's own keys through it: `timeout` (the per-call deadline), `fetch` (the transport), `headers` (the per-request header hook), and `format` (the context-framing default). [`agent.md`](agent.md) states each one.\r\n87: \r\n88: The wire leaves - `mapMessages` and the `extract*` narrowing set - are the pure request projection and response extractions `OllamaProvider` composes. They are exported and unit-tested on their own so the package's highest-risk code, the boundary narrowing of every wire `unknown` through the `@orkestrel/contract` guards, is provable without a daemon. Everything they do not do lives in `@orkestrel/agent`: the deadline, the transport seam, the streaming spine, the splitter, and the assembly of a `ProviderResult`.\r\n89: \r\n90: `OllamaProvider` declares `name` (`'ollama'`) and inherits the rest of its data members from the base: `id` is the stable per-instance trace label, and `format` is the context-framing default `OllamaOptions.format` supplies (see [Context framing](#context-framing)). Its wire seams are documented under [Methods](#methods), and the `generate` / `stream` boundary it inherits under `AgentProviderInterface` in [`agent.md`](agent.md). `AgentProvider` / `AgentProviderInterface` / `ProviderInterface` / `ProviderOptions` / `ProviderRequest` / `ProviderIncrement` / `ProviderParserInterface` / `ProviderResult` / `ProviderDelta` / `ProviderStreamOptions` / `ProviderError` / `isProviderError` / `ProviderAbortError` / `isProviderAbortError` / `Message` / `ContextFormat` are owned by `@orkestrel/agent`; `ToolDefinition` / `ToolCall` by `@orkestrel/tool`; the `TokenUsage` shape by `@orkestrel/budget`; `createNDJSONParser` by `@orkestrel/ndjson`. All are reused here, never redefined.\r\n91: \r\n92: ## Methods\r\n93: \r\n94: The wire seams `OllamaProvider` declares - exactly the members of `AgentProviderInterface` that the base leaves open. The runtime never calls them directly: a caller drives `generate` or `stream`, and the engine calls these to frame, project, decode, and drain the Ollama wire. Those inherited calls, the `id` / `name` / `format` data members, and the `ProviderParserInterface` the seam returns are documented in [`agent.md`](agent.md).\r\n95: \r\n96: #### `OllamaProvider`\r\n97: \r\n98: The `frame` method opens the call's framing state and the `finish` method drains it; the `body` method writes the request and the `read` method reads one record back.\r\n99: \r\n100: | Method   | Returns                                            | Summary                                                                        |\r\n101: | -------- | -------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |\r\n102: | `frame`  | `ProviderParserInterface`                          | Creates fresh NDJSON framing state for a call.                                 |\r\n103: | `body`   | `WireChatRequest`                                  | Projects conversation turns and per-call options onto the Ollama request body. |\r\n104: | `read`   | `ProviderIncrement`                                | Extracts a record's content, reasoning, tools, and completed usage report.     |\r\n105: | `finish` | `ReadonlyArray<Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>>` | Recovers a final NDJSON record that arrived without its line terminator.       |\r\n106: \r\n107: ## Contract\r\n108: \r\n109: These invariants hold across `src/core` \u001d `ollama.md`. The engine contract itself - `ProviderInterface`, `AgentProvider`, `ProviderResult`, `ProviderError`, `ProviderAbortError` - is in `@orkestrel/agent`, and [`agent.md`](agent.md) states it.\r\n110: \r\n111: 1. **Doc \u001d source bijection.** Every `function` / `class` / `const` / `interface` / `type` row in the `## Surface` table is a real export of the `src/core` surface, and every export appears as a Surface row - exhaustive, both directions.\r\n112: 2. **Imports each boundary from its owner, no cycle.** `OllamaProvider` extends `AgentProvider` and implements `AgentProviderInterface`; that base, `ProviderRequest`, `ProviderIncrement`, `ProviderParserInterface`, `ProviderInterface`, `ProviderOptions`, and `Message` come from `@orkestrel/agent`. `createNDJSONParser` comes from `@orkestrel/ndjson`, `ToolCall` from `@orkestrel/tool`, `TokenUsage` from `@orkestrel/budget`, and the guards (`isRecord` / `isString` / `isNumber` / `parseJSONAs`) from `@orkestrel/contract`. The provider errors are owned by `@orkestrel/agent` and `ToolDefinition` by `@orkestrel/tool`: `src/core` imports neither, and both reach a caller through the base - the base throws the errors this wire's failures become, and it accepts the `ToolDefinition[]` a call advertises. Each dependency is one-way and never imports from `@src/core`. No module under `src/` imports `@orkestrel/timeout` - the base arms the deadline - and the manifest declares it as a development dependency for the guide's bounding pattern, which imports it as a consumer would.\r\n113: 3. **The `/api/chat` wire body.** `OllamaProvider` POSTs to `${url}${OLLAMA_CHAT_PATH}`, defaulting `url` to `DEFAULT_OLLAMA_URL` and `keep_alive` to `DEFAULT_KEEP_ALIVE`. The body carries `model`, the mapped `messages`, `stream: true` on every call, `keep_alive`, and `think` (the per-call `ProviderStreamOptions.think` override when present, else `OllamaOptions.think`, default `false`), adding `options` only when configured, `format` only when the call supplies a `ProviderStreamOptions.schema`, and `tools` only when a non-empty `ToolDefinition[]` is passed. Each tool maps to `{ type: 'function', function: { name } }`, with `description` and `parameters` added only when the corresponding `ToolDefinition` fields are supplied. Messages map to the wire's minimal `{ role, content }` turn, with `tool_calls` added only on a turn that replays them and `images` only on a multimodal turn. A non-OK status is the base's failure, not this package's: it throws `ProviderError` with code `'HTTP'`, the response `status`, and a message bounded to an excerpt of the response body.\r\n114: 4. **Configurable `think` on the wire; the base's splitter keeps the assembled content clean.** For a thinking-capable model (for example `qwen3`) the per-request `think` flag is the only wire-level reasoning control (its native renderer honours neither the qwen3 `/no_think` token nor a Modelfile `PARAMETER think false`). It is configurable through `OllamaOptions.think` (default `false` - so the general-purpose provider stays immediate for non-thinking models and tests fast) and overrideable per call through `ProviderStreamOptions.think`. Set `think: true` when the caller displays reasoning separately from the answer: the daemon then separates reasoning natively, returning it on the distinct `message.thinking` channel, which `read` reports and the engine yields as `ProviderDelta` `{ channel: 'thinking', text }` and accumulates onto `ProviderResult.thinking`. Either way the base's `split` behaviour stays armed, because a daemon may ignore `think: false` for a thinking model and render reasoning inline as `<think>.</think>` content; `OllamaProvider` passes no `split` to `super`, so the default separation applies and the assembled `content` is the splitter's clean accumulation. The reasoning never re-enters the conversation.\r\n115: 5. **One NDJSON path.** Every call consumes NDJSON - one JSON object per `\\n`-terminated line. `frame()` returns `createNDJSONParser()`, fresh per call, so no call inherits another's half-read record, and the base pairs it with a streaming `TextDecoder` so a record split across byte reads is reassembled. At end of input the base calls `finish(parser)`, which feeds a trailing `\\n` through the parser: a non-conformant proxy's final unterminated line is recovered rather than silently dropped. `OllamaProvider` passes no `strict` to `super`, so a stream that ends without a settled record still assembles its result from the deltas it carried.\r\n116: 6. **`stream` yields deltas and returns the assembled result.** The engine yields each non-empty clean content delta as `{ channel: 'content', text }` and each daemon-side reasoning delta as `{ channel: 'thinking', text }`; its return value is the assembled `ProviderResult` whose `content` is the splitter's authoritative clean accumulation - the concatenation of the yielded content deltas, except across an implicit-open reclassification, where the reasoning prefix had already streamed before a bare `</think>` revealed it and the yields cannot be recalled - plus any tool calls collected across lines, the usage from the `done` line, and the separated `thinking` when the turn produced any.\r\n117: 7. **Usage from the `done` line (reuses `TokenUsage`).** `read` reports `usage` only for a record whose `done` is `true` and whose `prompt_eval_count` and `eval_count` are both numbers - mapped to `{ prompt, completion, total: prompt + completion }`, the `TokenUsage` shape, imported rather than redefined. A delta line carries no counts and a `done: false` line's counts are ignored, so neither contributes usage, and `ProviderResult.usage` is absent when the turn reported none.\r\n118: 8. **Tool-call extraction with id-generation (no `as`).** `result.tools` is the model's `message.tool_calls`, each entry narrowed to `{ id, name, arguments }`: the entry and its `function` must be records and `name` a string (else the entry is dropped); `arguments` is the wire object as-is, a JSON string parsed to a record, or `{}` when neither; `id` is the wire's `id` when a string, else a freshly minted `crypto.randomUUID()`. An empty result `tools` is never surfaced - its absence means \"no calls\".\r\n119: 9. **Boundary narrowing - all wire `unknown` through guards, never `as`.** Every value read off the wire (each NDJSON record, `message`, `content`, `thinking`, the usage counts, `tool_calls`, `arguments`) arrives as `unknown` and is narrowed through the `@orkestrel/contract` guards - never a type assertion. A missing or malformed field degrades to a sensible default (empty content, no usage, `{}` arguments), never a throw - the one external boundary kept honest.\r\n120: 10. **The base owns everything that is not the wire.** The per-call deadline (`OllamaOptions.timeout`, `120_000`ms when omitted, folded with the caller's signal so either cancels the request and the timer is always cleared), the cancellation rule (a `stream` cancelled mid-flight throws `ProviderAbortError` carrying the partial, and the reader and parser are released in a `finally`), the transport seam (`OllamaOptions.fetch` and the per-request `OllamaOptions.headers` hook, awaited inside the deadline and raced against the combined signal), and the bounded error read all live in `AgentProvider`. `OllamaOptions` extends `ProviderOptions`, so those keys are the base's and behave identically for every provider built on it; [`agent.md`](agent.md) states each rule once.\r\n121: 11. **Context-framing `format` (provider-default cascade level, expose-only).** `OllamaOptions.format` is an optional `ContextFormat` (from `@orkestrel/agent`) - the provider's context-framing default, passed to `super` and exposed as `provider.format`. It is the provider-default level of the `AgentContext` build cascade (beats the managers' built-in framing, beaten by a manager-options or per-item override; see [`agent.md`](agent.md)), read by the Agent when it assembles the prompt. Omitted ? `undefined` (framing-agnostic; core's built-in framing applies unchanged). It is **never sent on the `/api/chat` wire**: it is consumed by core, absent from the request `body`, and unrelated to Ollama's structured-output `format` parameter - that one is sent in the request `body`, but only when a per-call `ProviderStreamOptions.schema` is supplied - the framing default and that wire parameter only share a word.\r\n122: 12. **Event-free.** A pure functional boundary - no Emitter, no events. Each call is a function of its arguments.\r\n123: 13. **A core face that runs in a browser.** The package publishes the `.` entry alone, built from `src/core`, which imports no `node:*` module and no DOM global; the scoped core typecheck compiles it with the `ESNext` and `WebWorker` libraries and no ambient `@types`, so neither Node's globals nor the DOM's are in scope, and the base binds `globalThis.fetch` to its own receiver so a browser does not throw `Illegal invocation` on a bare transport reference. On 2026-09-14 in Chrome 148 the built `@orkestrel/ollama` core entry and its whole `@orkestrel` import closure loaded as ES modules with no console error: `OllamaProvider` drove the local daemon directly for a settled answer carrying its usage counts, a cancel mid-stream returned `ProviderAbortError` holding the partial that had streamed, and the same page reached that daemon through a `createRelay` server, where a wrong bearer arrived as `ProviderError` with code `'HTTP'` and status `401`. The daemon is the remaining limit - a page reaches `/api/chat` directly only where the daemon, or a proxy in front of it, answers the page's origin; otherwise relay the call through your own server ([Relaying through your own server](#relaying-through-your-own-server)).\r\n124: 14. **Tested live against a real local Ollama (no `skipIf`).** The live tests run against a real Ollama daemon - no mocks, only genuine third-party calls - model `qwen3.5:2b-q4_K_M`, with `OLLAMA_HOST` / `OLLAMA_MODEL` overridable. Unlike the other surfaces, the dedicated `service` project requires the daemon: `tests/setupService.ts` throws a clear error if it is unreachable and warms the model (a `num_predict: 1` chat) before the suite, so the live tests run unconditionally (no `describe.skipIf`). The project runs serially (`fileParallelism: false`) with a 120s test/hook timeout so a cold load cannot flake it; `keep_alive` keeps the model resident across files, and `bash scripts/ollama.sh` brings the daemon and model up before the battery in CI. Assertions are structural - they hold whatever wording a small model produces - and never pin exact output. The hermetic half sits in `tests/src/core/`, and the relay round trip is proved hermetically in [`tests/src/core/integration.test.ts`](../tests/src/core/integration.test.ts) and live in [`tests/service/relay.test.ts`](../tests/service/relay.test.ts).\r\n125: 15. **Doc \u001d source method bijection.** The `## Methods` table lists exactly the members `OllamaProvider` declares - `frame`, `body`, `read`, and `finish` - and the class declares exactly those. `generate` and `stream` reach a caller through the same class but are declared once, in `AgentProvider`, and documented in [`agent.md`](agent.md).\r\n126: \r\n127: ## Patterns\r\n128: \r\n129: ### `createOllama` + `generate`\r\n130: \r\n131: The dominant single-shot use: one prompt, one assembled result.\r\n132: \r\n133: ```ts\r\n134: import { createAbort } from '@orkestrel/abort'\r\n135: import { createOllama } from '@orkestrel/ollama'\r\n136: \r\n137: const provider = createOllama({ model: 'qwen3.5:2b-q4_K_M', options: { temperature: 0 } })\r\n138: const abort = createAbort()\r\n139: const messages = [\r\n140: \t{ id: '1', role: 'user', content: 'Summarize the release notes for version 2.0.' },\r\n141: ] as const\r\n142: \r\n143: const result = await provider.generate(messages, abort.signal)\r\n144: console.log(result.content)\r\n145: if (result.usage) charge(result.usage) // fold into a token budget\r\n146: ```\r\n147: \r\n148: ### Bounding a call with a budget + timeout\r\n149: \r\n150: `generate` / `stream` take a plain `AbortSignal`, so fold an abort, a timeout, and a token budget into one bound through `AbortSignal.any` - whichever trips first cancels the call. This caller-side bound stacks on top of the base's own armed deadline (`OllamaOptions.timeout`, `120_000`ms when omitted): the request dies when either fires, so you get an external cancel or budget and a hard per-call ceiling together.\r\n151: \r\n152: ```ts\r\n153: import { createAbort } from '@orkestrel/abort'\r\n154: import { createTimeout } from '@orkestrel/timeout'\r\n155: import { createTokenBudget } from '@orkestrel/budget'\r\n156: import { createOllama } from '@orkestrel/ollama'\r\n157: \r\n158: const provider = createOllama({ model: 'qwen3.5:2b-q4_K_M' })\r\n159: const messages = [{ id: '1', role: 'user', content: 'Say hello.' }] as const\r\n160: const abort = createAbort() // external cancel\r\n161: const timeout = createTimeout({ ms: 30_000 }) // wall-clock deadline\r\n162: const budget = createTokenBudget({ max: 50_000, scope: 'total' }) // cost ceiling\r\n163: timeout.start()\r\n164: budget.start()\r\n165: \r\n166: const bound = AbortSignal.any([abort.signal, timeout.signal, budget.signal])\r\n167: const result = await provider.generate(messages, bound)\r\n168: budget.consume(result.usage ?? { prompt: 0, completion: 0, total: 0 })\r\n169: ```\r\n170: \r\n171: ### Projecting the wire without a daemon\r\n172: \r\n173: The seam members are ordinary methods, so a test or a debugging session can read the exact request this provider would send and the exact contribution one daemon record makes - with no socket, no daemon, and no model. Construct `OllamaProvider` directly: `createOllama` returns the narrower `ProviderInterface`, which exposes the call boundary alone.\r\n174: \r\n175: ```ts\r\n176: import { OllamaProvider } from '@orkestrel/ollama'\r\n177: \r\n178: const provider = new OllamaProvider({ model: 'qwen3.5:2b-q4_K_M', keepAlive: '9m' })\r\n179: const request = provider.body({\r\n180: \tmessages: [{ id: '1', role: 'user', content: 'Say hello.' }],\r\n181: \toptions: { think: true },\r\n182: })\r\n183: request.stream // true - every call streams\r\n184: request.think // true - the per-call override beats the constructed default\r\n185: request.keep_alive // '9m'\r\n186: request.messages // [{ role: 'user', content: 'Say hello.' }] - ids stay local\r\n187: \r\n188: const parser = provider.frame()\r\n189: parser.parse('{\"message\":{\"content\":\"Hel\"}}\\n') // [{ message: { content: 'Hel' } }]\r\n190: provider.read({ message: { content: 'lo' }, done: true, prompt_eval_count: 3, eval_count: 4 })\r\n191: // { content: 'lo', thinking: '', tools: [], usage: { prompt: 3, completion: 4, total: 7 } }\r\n192: provider.finish(parser) // [] - the parser held nothing back\r\n193: ```\r\n194: \r\n195: ### Running in the browser\r\n196: \r\n197: The published face is core, so the same `createOllama` call runs unchanged in a browser module. Point `url` at whatever the page can actually reach: the daemon's own origin where it answers that origin, or your own server standing in front of it. A page that reaches a daemon directly is a development arrangement - it puts the model endpoint on the network the page runs on - so prefer one of the server patterns that follow for anything a user runs.\r\n198: \r\n199: ```ts\r\n200: import { createOllama } from '@orkestrel/ollama'\r\n=== npm run lint\r\nnpm notice run @orkestrel/ollama@0.0.15 lint\r\nnpm notice run oxlint --config .oxlintrc.json --fix .\r\n=== exit=0\r\n=== npm run format\r\nnpm notice run @orkestrel/ollama@0.0.15 format\r\nnpm notice run oxfmt --config .oxfmtrc.json --write .\r\nFinished in 1710ms on 65 files using 16 threads.\r\n=== exit=0\r\n=== npm run format:check\r\nnpm notice run @orkestrel/ollama@0.0.15 format:check\r\nnpm notice run oxfmt --config .oxfmtrc.json --check .\r\nChecking formatting...\r\n\r\nAll matched files use the correct format.\r\nFinished in 1571ms on 65 files using 16 threads.\r\n=== exit=0\r\n=== npm run lint:check\r\nnpm notice run @orkestrel/ollama@0.0.15 lint:check\r\nnpm notice run oxlint --config .oxlintrc.json --deny-warnings .\r\n=== exit=0\r\n=== npm run check\r\nnpm notice run @orkestrel/ollama@0.0.15 check\r\nnpm notice run tsc --noEmit What a caller drives — `generate`, `stream`, `id`, `format` — is the base's, and [`agent.md`](agent.md) documents it.\n \n The design is deliberately spare — an external boundary kept honest. Every `unknown` wire value is narrowed through the `@orkestrel/contract` guards (`isRecord`, `isString`, `isNumber`) rather than a type assertion, and a missing or malformed field degrades to a sensible default (empty content, no usage, `{}` arguments) rather than a throw. Every call streams: the request always carries `stream: true`, and `generate` drains the same NDJSON path `stream` exposes, so neither call can report content the other would not. The wire `think` flag is configurable through `OllamaOptions.think` (default `false`) and overrideable per call through `ProviderStreamOptions.think`; with `think: true` the daemon returns reasoning on the separate `message.thinking` channel, streamed live as `thinking` deltas. Either way the base's splitter separates any `<think>` span the daemon inlines anyway, so the assembled `content` is clean and the reasoning surfaces as `ProviderResult.thinking`, never in the conversation. A per-call `ProviderStreamOptions.schema` (a JSON schema object, from `@orkestrel/agent`) forwards verbatim as the wire's structured-output `format` field, and is omitted from the request when no schema is supplied. The body carries `model`, the mapped `messages`, `stream: true` on every call, `keep_alive`, and `think` (the per-call `ProviderStreamOptions.think` override when present, else `OllamaOptions.think`, default `false`), adding `options` only when configured, `format` only when the call supplies a `ProviderStreamOptions.schema`, and `tools` only when a non-empty `ToolDefinition[]` is passed. Each tool maps to `{ type: 'function', function: { name } }`, with `description` and `parameters` added only when the corresponding `ToolDefinition` fields are supplied. Messages map to the wire's minimal `{ role, content }` turn, with `tool_calls` added only on a turn that replays them and `images` only on a multimodal turn. A non-OK status is the base's failure, not this package's: it throws `ProviderError` with code `'HTTP'`, the response `status`, and a message bounded to an excerpt of the response body.\n 4. **Configurable `think` on the wire; the base's splitter keeps the assembled content clean.** For a thinking-capable model (for example `qwen3`) the per-request `think` flag is the only wire-level reasoning control (its native renderer honours neither the qwen3 `/no_think` token nor a Modelfile `PARAMETER think false`). It is configurable through `OllamaOptions.think` (default `false` — so the general-purpose provider stays immediate for non-thinking models and tests fast) and overrideable per call through `ProviderStreamOptions.think`. Set `think: true` when the caller displays reasoning separately from the answer: the daemon then separates reasoning natively, returning it on the distinct `message.thinking` channel, which `read` reports and the engine yields as `ProviderDelta` `{ channel: 'thinking', text }` and accumulates onto `ProviderResult.thinking`. Either way the base's `split` behaviour stays armed, because a daemon may ignore `think: false` for a thinking model and render reasoning inline as `<think>…</think>` content; `OllamaProvider` passes no `split` to `super`, so the default separation applies and the assembled `content` is the splitter's clean accumulation. The reasoning never re-enters the conversation.\n 5. **One NDJSON path.** Every call consumes NDJSON — one JSON object per `\\n`-terminated line. `frame()` returns `createNDJSONParser()`, fresh per call, so no call inherits another's half-read record, and the base pairs it with a streaming `TextDecoder` so a record split across byte reads is reassembled. At end of input the base calls `finish(parser)`, which feeds a trailing `\\n` through the parser: a non-conformant proxy's final unterminated line is recovered rather than silently dropped. `OllamaProvider` passes no `strict` to `super`, so a stream that ends without a settled record still assembles its result from the deltas it carried.\n 6. **`stream` yields deltas and returns the assembled result.** The engine yields each non-empty clean content delta as `{ channel: 'content', text }` and each daemon-side reasoning delta as `{ channel: 'thinking', text }`; its return value is the assembled `ProviderResult` whose `content` is the splitter's authoritative clean accumulation — the concatenation of the yielded content deltas, except across an implicit-open reclassification, where the reasoning prefix had already streamed before a bare `</think>` revealed it and the yields cannot be recalled — plus any tool calls collected across lines, the usage from the `done` line, and the separated `thinking` when the turn produced any.\n@@ -113,9 +118,9 @@ These invariants hold across `src/core` ↔ `ollama.md`. Each is a pure, total function of its parameters: a missing\r\n// or malformed wire field degrades to a sensible default (empty content, no usage, `{}`\r\n// arguments), never a throw, and no value is reached through `as`.\r\n\r\nimport type { Message } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\nimport type { TokenUsage } from '@orkestrel/budget'\r\nimport type { ToolCall } from '@orkestrel/tool'\r\nimport type { WireChatRequest } from './types.js'\r\nimport { isNumber, isRecord, isString, parseJSONAs } from '@orkestrel/contract'\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Maps conversation turns onto the `/api/chat` wire's minimal message shape.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * `tool_calls` is emitted only on a turn that replays them and `images` only on a\r\n * multimodal turn, so an empty optional never reaches the wire.\r\n *\r\n * @param messages - The conversation turns to send\r\n * @returns The wire `messages` array, one entry per turn, in order\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * mapMessages([{ id: '1', role: 'user', content: 'Say hello.' }])\r\n * // [{ role: 'user', content: 'Say hello.' }]\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function mapMessages(messages: readonly Message[]): WireChatRequest['messages'] {\r\n\treturn messages.map((message) => ({\r\n\t\trole: message.role,\r\n\t\tcontent: message.content,\r\n\t\t...(message.calls !== undefined && message.calls.length > 0\r\n\t\t\t? { images: [...message.images] }\r\n\t\t\t: {}),\r\n\t}))\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Extracts the assistant text of one wire record.\r\n *\r\n * @param record - One parsed `/api/chat` NDJSON record\r\n * @returns The record's `message.content` when it is a string, else `''`\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * extractContent({ message: { content: 'ok' } }) // 'ok'\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function extractContent(record: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>): string {\r\n\tconst message = Reflect.get(record, 'message')\r\n\tif (!isRecord(message)) return ''\r\n\tconst content = Reflect.get(message, 'content')\r\n\treturn isString(content) ? content : ''\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Extracts the daemon-side reasoning of one wire record.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * `message.thinking` is the `think: true` wire shape. It is read whatever the configured\r\n * flag says, because a daemon may separate reasoning on its own.\r\n *\r\n * @param record - One parsed `/api/chat` NDJSON record\r\n * @returns The record's `message.thinking` when it is a string, else `''`\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * extractThinking({ message: { thinking: 'weighing it' } }) // 'weighing it'\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function extractThinking(record: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>): string {\r\n\tconst message = Reflect.get(record, 'message')\r\n\tif (!isRecord(message)) return ''\r\n\tconst thinking = Reflect.get(message, 'thinking')\r\n\treturn isString(thinking) ? thinking : ''\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Extracts the token usage of one wire record.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * Both counts must be numbers, which is true of the stream's `done: true` line. A\r\n * delta line carries neither, so it yields `undefined`.\r\n *\r\n * @param record - One parsed `/api/chat` NDJSON record\r\n * @returns The `TokenUsage` shape, or `undefined` when either count is absent\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * extractUsage({ prompt_eval_count: 3, eval_count: 4 })\r\n * // { prompt: 3, completion: 4, total: 7 }\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function extractUsage(record: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>): TokenUsage | undefined {\r\n\tconst prompt = Reflect.get(record, 'prompt_eval_count')\r\n\tconst completion = Reflect.get(record, 'eval_count')\r\n\tif (!isNumber(prompt) || !isNumber(completion)) return undefined\r\n\treturn { prompt, completion, total: prompt + completion }\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Extracts the tool calls of one wire record's `message.tool_calls`.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * Each entry narrows to `{ id, name, arguments }`: the entry and its `function` must be\r\n * records and `name` a string, else the entry is dropped. An id is minted when the wire\r\n * omits one.\r\n *\r\n * @param record - One parsed `/api/chat` NDJSON record\r\n * @returns The narrowed tool calls, empty when the record carries none\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * extractTools({ message: { tool_calls: [{ function: { name: 'weather' } }] } })\r\n * // [{ id: '.', name: 'weather', arguments: {} }]\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function extractTools(record: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>): readonly ToolCall[] {\r\n\tconst message = Reflect.get(record, 'message')\r\n\tif (!isRecord(message)) return []\r\n\tconst calls = Reflect.get(message, 'tool_calls')\r\n\tif (!Array.isArray(calls)) return []\r\n\tconst out: ToolCall[] = []\r\n\tfor (const entry of calls) {\r\n\t\tif (!isRecord(entry)) continue\r\n\t\tconst callable = Reflect.get(entry, 'function')\r\n\t\tif (!isRecord(callable)) continue\r\n\t\tconst name = Reflect.get(callable, 'name')\r\n\t\tif (!isString(name)) continue\r\n\t\tconst id = Reflect.get(entry, 'id')\r\n\t\tout.push({\r\n\t\t\tid: isString(id) ? id : crypto.randomUUID(),\r\n\t\t\tname,\r\n\t\t\targuments: extractArguments(Reflect.get(callable, 'arguments')),\r\n\t\t})\r\n\t}\r\n\treturn out\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Extracts a wire `arguments` value as a record.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * Total: an object passes through, a JSON string is parsed when it yields a record, and\r\n * a malformed string yields `{}` rather than throwing.\r\n *\r\n * @param value - The wire's `function.arguments` value, of unknown shape\r\n * @returns The argument record, or `{}` when the value carries none\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * extractArguments('{\"city\":\"Oslo\"}') // { city: 'Oslo' }\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function extractArguments(value: unknown): Readonly<Record<string, unknown>> {\r\n\tif (isRecord(value)) return value\r\n\tif (isString(value)) return parseJSONAs(value, isRecord) ??
