Each call takes an\r\n * `AbortSignal` to bound the request; a `stream` cancelled mid-flight throws a\r\n * `ProviderAbortError` carrying the partial result.\r\n *\r\n * The optional `fetch` + `headers` form a transport seam (see {@link OllamaOptions}):\r\n * point `url` at your own server, inject a custom `fetch`, and have `headers` attach a\r\n * generated/obfuscated bearer token your server validates - so a browser runtime\r\n * reaches the LLM through your middleware without this library ever handling the real API\r\n * key. Both omitted ? the global `fetch` and only a JSON content type.\r\n *\r\n * The optional `format` is the provider's context-framing default - the provider-default\r\n * level of `AgentContext`'s format cascade (beaten by a manager-options or per-item\r\n * override, beating the managers' built-in framing), declaring how this\r\n * provider's models prefer context sections framed (for example XML group wrappers vs. Markdown\r\n * headers). It is exposed on the provider for the Agent's `build()` and is not Ollama's\r\n * `/api/chat` `format` wire parameter (structured output) - the framing default and that\r\n * wire parameter are unrelated despite the shared word. Omitted ? the provider is\r\n * framing-agnostic (core's built-in defaults).\r\n *\r\n * @param options - `model` (required), and optional `url` / `keepAlive` / `timeout` /\r\n *   `options` / `fetch` / `headers` / `format` (see {@link OllamaOptions})\r\n * @returns A working {@link ProviderInterface} backed by Ollama\r\n *\r\n * @example createOllama + generate\r\n * ```ts\r\n * import { provider.generate(messages, abort.signal)\r\n * console.log(result.content)\r\n * if (result.usage) charge(result.usage) // fold into a token budget\r\n * ```\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * Route through This file adds only what TypeScript itself decides.\r\n\r\n## Syntax and imports\r\n\r\n- Tabs for indentation.\r\n- No semicolons unless ASI requires one.\r\n- Single quotes.\r\n- Named exports only, except framework-required defaults.\r\n- ESM imports use explicit `.js` extensions for local TypeScript modules.\r\n- Place `import type` declarations before value imports.\r\n- Do not place blank lines between consecutive imports of the same kind.\r\n- Narrow an accepted `unknown` with a total guard rather than a conditional access.\r\n- A parameter property is a constructor parameter carrying any accessibility or `readonly` modifier.\r\n  The ban reaches every form, `readonly` alone included; declare the `#` field and assign it in the\r\n  constructor body.\r\n\r\n## Types\r\n\r\n- Put every reusable or public interface/type alias in the nearest authoritative `*/types.ts`.\r\n- Public collection properties and return types use `readonly T[]`, `ReadonlyMap<K, V>`, or `ReadonlySet<T>`.\r\n- Optional state is `T | undefined`; an optional lookup failure returns `undefined`.\r\n- `as const` annotates a literal with its own type and never overrides the checker, so the assertion\r\n  ban does not reach it. Use it to derive a literal union from a value and to fix a tuple's arity and\r\n  element types. Do not write it on a value whose contract is already declared; annotate the\r\n  declaration instead. A class field that holds one literal keeps `as const`\r\n  (`readonly code = 'ABORT' as const`): the vendored lint gate's `prefer-as-const` rule refuses the\r\n  annotated form, and a field whose type is a union of literals is annotated as the preceding\r\n  sentence states.\r\n\r\n## Immutability\r\n\r\n- Never mutate caller-owned inputs.\r\n- Use copy-on-write for internal state.\r\n- Return copies or readonly views; never leak a mutable internal reference.\r\n- Compute derived facts instead of persisting duplicate state.\r\n\r\n## Errors and outcomes\r\n\r\n| Condition                            | Required strategy                           |\r\n| ------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------- |\r\n| Programmer error or invalid argument | Throw an `AppError`                         |\r\n| I/O/network/external operation       | Return `Result<T, E>` or throw consistently |\r\n| Optional missing lookup              | Return `undefined`                          |\r\n| Invalid input inside a guard         | Return `false`; never throw                 |\r\n\r\nUse the existing outcome contract from a declared dependency or the owning environment; do not redeclare it at each call site. When no such contract exists and adding a dependency is not authorized, define this once in the owning `types.ts`:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\ninterface Success<T> {\r\n\treadonly success: true\r\n\treadonly value: T\r\n}\r\n\r\ninterface Failure<E> {\r\n\treadonly success: false\r\n\treadonly error: E\r\n}\r\n\r\ntype Result<T, E = Error> = Success<T> | Failure<E>\r\n```\r\n\r\n- When `@orkestrel/contract` is declared, inspect and use its exact installed `Result`, construction/narrowing helpers, and `attempt` behavior instead of duplicating them.\r\n- Otherwise construct and narrow through the owning environment's centralized helpers.\r\n- Error classes expose a machine-readable `code` and optional `context`.\r\n- Every public error class ships with a guard such as `isAppError` for safe `catch` narrowing.\r\n\r\n## Comments and API documentation\r\n\r\n- Comments explain why, never restate what self-explanatory code does. The Writing rules in `AGENTS.md` and `.claude/rules/writing.md` govern their prose.\r\n- Every public export has complete TSDoc: description, `@param`, `@returns`, and `@example` where applicable.\r\n- The first sentence states what the symbol does in the third person with an `-s` verb - `Creates`,\r\n  `Returns`, `Checks whether` - and never repeats the symbol's name.\r\n- `policy/no-malformed-summary` reads that first sentence over every doc block a top-level export\r\n  declaration follows, and refuses an opening word that is not a third-person `-s` verb and a\r\n  sentence naming the declared symbol. A word ending in `s` that the rule's stop set does not name\r\n  passes whether or not it is a verb - a plural noun such as `Files` included - so read the sentence\r\n  in review as well.\r\n- Describe a boolean parameter as \"If `true`, .; if `false`, .\", and a boolean return as\r\n  \"True if .; false otherwise\".\r\n- Write a default as \"Default: .\" and a thrown error as \"Thrown when .\".\r\n- State a prerequisite and the failure behavior wherever the symbol has either.\r\n- `@deprecated` names the replacement first, then the reason.\r\n- Document an options object as one `@param`; describe its short fields under `@remarks`.\r\n- Private methods and overload-specific notes use single-line `//` comments, not public TSDoc.\r\n- Do not document speculative future product behavior unless requested.\r\n- Never use `@internal` to hide a method; make it `#` private.\r\n---\r\npaths:\r\n  - '**/*.{ts,tsx,mts,cts,vue}'\r\n---\r\n\r\n# Naming and API-shape rules\r\n\r\nNames are public API. A consumer can predict them without documentation.\r\n\r\n## Entity-scoped names: one word\r\n\r\nProperties/getters, methods, option keys, and event names belong to an entity whose type already supplies context. Use one descriptive word:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\ninterface AgentInterface {\r\n\treadonly id: string\r\n\treadonly context: AgentContextInterface\r\n\treadonly status: AgentStatus\r\n\tgenerate(): Promise<string>\r\n\tstream(options?: AgentStreamOptions): AsyncGenerator<string, string>\r\n\tabort(): void\r\n}\r\n```\r\n\r\nHard targets:\r\n\r\n- Public properties: one word.\r\n- Public methods: one word; use two only when no single verb is accurate.\r\n- Ungrouped option keys: one word.\r\n- Grouped option key: the configured entity noun; every leaf remains one word.\r\n- Events: one present-tense verb or noun.\r\n- Private methods: two or three words are acceptable.\r\n\r\nThe rule does not apply to standalone helpers, type names with role suffixes, or qualified constants.\r\n\r\n## Split instead of compounding\r\n\r\nA compound entity member usually means the API contains multiple entities or behaviors. Do not create `addTool`, `removeDocument`, `databasePath`, or `serverTimeout`.\r\n\r\n### Group options by entity\r\n\r\n```ts\r\ninterface ServerOptions {\r\n\treadonly server?: { readonly port?: number; readonly timeout?: number }\r\n\treadonly database?: { readonly path?: string; readonly timeout?: number }\r\n}\r\n```\r\n\r\nNever flatten these into prefixed keys.\r\n\r\n### Extract sub-entities\r\n\r\nMove prefixed method families to a manager and expose it as a noun:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\ninterface AgentContextInterface {\r\n\treadonly instructions: InstructionManagerInterface\r\n\treadonly documents: DocumentManagerInterface\r\n\treadonly tools: ToolManagerInterface\r\n}\r\n```\r\n\r\nCall `context.instructions.add(input)`, not `context.addInstruction(input)`.\r\n\r\n### Split behavioral variants\r\n\r\nDo not hide multiple algorithms behind a discriminator parameter:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nparseJSON(input)\r\nparseYAML(input)\r\nparseTOML(input)\r\n```\r\n\r\nDo not write `parse(input, format)`. A literal that selects a different action is a magic mode and requires separate functions/methods.\r\n\r\nDistinguish behavior from data:\r\n\r\n- Different value selects a different action/algorithm/shape of work \u001a split.\r\n- Different value selects a datum for the same operation \u001a keep it as data.\r\n- Discriminants, reasoning/error codes, uniformly applied value enums, and field/key selectors are data.\r\n\r\n## Standalone helpers\r\n\r\nModule helpers have no owning entity at the call site, so default to `{verb}{Noun}`:\r\n\r\n`generateId`, `computeHash`, `extractColumns`, `derivePhaseStatus`, `inferLanguage`, `normalizePath`, `sanitizeFilename`, `matchesGlobPattern`, `belongsTo`, `hasMany`.\r\n\r\n- A one-word helper is valid only when its meaning and arguments are unmistakable: `delay`, `clamp`, `tokenize`, `similarity`.\r\n- Reject vague helpers such as `process` or `handle`.\r\n- A helper prefix has one project-wide meaning:\r\n  - `extract*` extracts structure.\r\n  - `infer*` derives.\r\n  - `compute*` calculates deterministically.\r\n  - `matches*` is a predicate.\r\n  - `build*` assembles a composite value from parts and is neither a factory nor a combinator named for its constituents; see `create*` and `*Of` in \u0015 Fixed derivation/construction forms.\r\n  - `read*` obtains a value from a live host object, a stream position, or a byte layout, returns it or throws, and never coerces; a coercing helper is `parse*` in \u0015 Fixed derivation/construction forms.\r\n  - `resolve*` picks the effective value from options and defaults.\r\n  - `scan*` walks a structure and returns its findings.\r\n  - `describe*` takes a finding and returns the human-readable message that names it.\r\n  - `normalize*` returns the canonical form of a value of the same type.\r\n  - `collect*` gathers members into a collection.\r\n  - `filter*` returns the members of a collection that satisfy a predicate, in order, and never mutates its input.\r\n  - `render*` produces text or markup from a value that is not a finding.\r\n  - `supports*` is a capability predicate and narrows no type.\r\n- When a helper family grows around one shape, promote it to a class with entity-scoped one-word methods.\r\n\r\n## General vocabulary\r\n\r\nThe root design laws in `AGENTS.md` - one term per concept, boolean behavior switches, `undefined` absence, derived state, real domain states, and named discriminants - bind here. This section adds only their naming specifics:\r\n\r\n- Describe what a thing is, not its implementation.\r\n- Prefer short common English; avoid jargon, abbreviations, and non-universal acronyms.\r\n- Properties are nouns; methods are verbs.\r\n- Booleans read as assertions: `aborted`, `exhausted`, `expired`.\r\n- Accessors use bare nouns, never `get*`/`set*`.\r\n- Do not alternate `count`/`length`/`size`/`total` or `abort`/`cancel`.\r\n- Name the axis a discriminant varies: `relationship`, `command`, `category`, `operation`, `via`.\r\n- A binary switch is a boolean such as `bail`, never `'continue' | 'halt'`; genuine discriminants, multi-state lifecycles, conventional value pairs (`ascending`/`descending`, `and`/`or`), and external-spec literals remain unions.\r\n- An option key, constant, or member that transliterates an external protocol field, format field, or engine pragma keeps the external wording in this project's casing, and its TSDoc names the source it mirrors: the `foreignKeys` key mirrors the `PRAGMA foreign_keys` statement, and the `keepAlive` key mirrors the Ollama `keep_alive` field.\r\n- Outside a declared wire body, a mirrored name never uses `kind` or `type` as a member name, and never uses a word \u0015 Rejected naming lists. A Compound File Binary (CFB) directory entry's object-type byte takes a named discriminant.\r\n- A declared wire body - a type whose members transliterate an external wire format field for field - keeps the external field names, `type` and `kind` included, and its TSDoc names the format it transliterates. The package's own domain type carries neither word, and the package owns the projection between the wire body and the domain type.\r\n\r\n## Acronyms\r\n\r\nKeep canonical case:\r\n\r\n- Initialisms: `JSON`, `HTTP`, `NDJSON`, `SSE`, `URL`, `URI`, `SQL`, `API`, `DB`, `TTL`, `UUID`, `RFC`, `JWT`.\r\n- Brands/proper names: `SQLite`, `IndexedDB`, `GitHub`, `OAuth`.\r\n- PascalCase identifiers preserve the acronym: `JSONSchema`, `HTTPServer`, `NDJSONParser`, `SSEStream`, `URLPattern`, `SQLiteDriver`, `IndexedDBDriver`.\r\n- camelCase lowers an acronym only when it leads: `jsonValue`, `urlPath`, `sqlText`; otherwise preserve it: `parseJSON`, `toJSON`, `fromNDJSON`, `signJWT`, `compileSQL`.\r\n- Ecosystem-conventional `id` and leading `url`/`uri` remain lowercase.\r\n- Domain folders remain lowercase: `http/`, `sqlite/`, `indexeddb/`, `websocket/`.\r\n- Never title-fold canonical acronyms into `Json`, `Ndjson`, `Http`, `Sse`, or `Sqlite`.\r\n\r\n## Type-level identifiers\r\n\r\n| Kind                      | Required form                           |\r\n| ------------------------- | --------------------------------------- |\r\n| Behavioral interface      | `{Entity}Interface`                     |\r\n| Options/config            | `{Entity}Options`                       |\r\n| Creation input            | `{Entity}Input`                         |\r\n| Outcome/output            | `{Entity}Result`                        |\r\n| Execution context         | `{Entity}Context`                       |\r\n| Event map                 | `{Entity}EventMap`                      |\r\n| Union/enum-like           | `{Entity}{Noun}`                        |\r\n| Plain non-behavioral data | `{Entity}`                              |\r\n| Function type             | `{Entity}Handler` or `{Entity}Function` |\r\n| Manager interface         | `{Entity}ManagerInterface`              |\r\n\r\n- Never prefix interfaces with `I`.\r\n- Never pluralize type names.\r\n- `Handler` is for function types, never classes.\r\n\r\n## Value-level identifiers\r\n\r\n| Kind           | Required form                         |\r\n| -------------- | ------------------------------------- |\r\n| Class          | PascalCase `{Entity}`                 |\r\n| Manager class  | PascalCase `{Entity}Manager`          |\r\n| Factory        | camelCase `create{Entity}`            |\r\n| Guard          | camelCase `is{Condition}`             |\r\n| Helper         | camelCase `{verb}{Noun}`              |\r\n| Constant       | UPPER_SNAKE_CASE `{QUALIFIER}_{NOUN}` |\r\n| Property/field | camelCase bare noun                   |\r\n| Method         | camelCase bare verb                   |\r\n| Boolean        | camelCase adjective/past participle   |\r\n\r\n## Fixed derivation/construction forms\r\n\r\n- A form's contract binds a new name; `.claude/rules/architecture.md` \u0015 Kind purity names the retained names that keep a form outside its file, such as `createWriteDirectory` and `isVacant`.\r\n- `is*`: total `Guard<T>`; never throws; returns false off-shape.\r\n- `parse*`: coercion producing `T | undefined`; cross-type conversion never belongs in a guard.\r\n- `create*`: the factory form; `.claude/rules/architecture.md` \u0015 Kind purity states what a factory is and where it lives.\r\n- `*Of`: combinator named for its constituents, combining them into a container/guard/value, such as `arrayOf(guard)` or `boundsOf(min, max)`.\r\n- `{noun}To{Noun}`: projection from a whole to a derived view, such as `definitionToSnapshot`.\r\n- `*Shape`: `ContractShape` value/JSON-Schema blueprint, not a function or type.\r\n- Leading `_`: intentionally unused binding only; never privacy.\r\n\r\nFor `_` bindings:\r\n\r\n- Use only for genuine callback/signature conformance, rest omission, swallowed catches, or intentionally unused loop variables.\r\n- Verify each use is intentional; remove `_` and wire the value the code must consume.\r\n- Remove the parameter when signature compatibility does not require it.\r\n- Prefer a short justification for each rare `_` in `src/`.\r\n\r\n## Tallies\r\n\r\n- A lone unambiguous tally is `count`.\r\n- When several distinct tallies coexist, name each fact: a pool may expose `size`, `idle`, and `active`; a queue may expose `count` and `active`.\r\n- Do not expose several ambiguous `count` properties.\r\n\r\n## Files and folders\r\n\r\n| Kind            | Pattern                                                                          |\r\n| --------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\r\n| Domain folder   | lowercase plural entity: `agents/`, `tools/`                                     |\r\n| Implementation  | PascalCase entity: `Agent.ts`                                                    |\r\n| Function module | camelCase function: `renderGrid.ts`                                              |\r\n| Test            | source filename without extension + `.test`: `Agent.test.ts`, `useTheme.test.ts` |\r\n| Guide           | lowercase domain: `agents.md`                                                    |\r\n\r\n## Fixed lifecycle vocabulary\r\n\r\n| Verb      | Exact meaning                             |\r\n| --------- | ----------------------------------------- |\r\n| `start`   | Begin or restart                          |\r\n| `stop`    | End permanently                           |\r\n| `pause`   | Suspend resumably                         |\r\n| `resume`  | Continue after pause                      |\r\n| `skip`    | Mark intentionally unexecuted             |\r\n| `abort`   | Cancel with signal propagation            |\r\n| `clear`   | Reset state without destroying the entity |\r\n| `destroy` | Tear down and release resources           |\r\n| `execute` | Run primary work to completion            |\r\n\r\nNever introduce synonyms such as `cancel`, `reset`, or `run` for these meanings.\r\n\r\n## Rejected naming\r\n\r\n- Generic words: `data`, `info`, `item`, `thing`, `obj`.\r\n- Type-encoded names: `nameString`, `countNumber`.\r\n- Abbreviations: `cfg`, `doc`, `msg`; write `config`, `document`, `message`.\r\n- `@internal` methods; use `#` privacy.\r\n- Compound entity members where grouping/extraction/splitting provides the context.\r\n- Behavior-selecting magic strings.\r\n---\r\npaths:\r\n  - 'src/**/*'\r\n  - 'app/**/*'\r\n  - 'configs/**/*'\r\n  - 'tests/**/*'\r\n  - 'scripts/**/*'\r\n  - 'guides/**/*'\r\n  - 'package.json'\r\n  - '.gitattributes'\r\n  - '.github/workflows/*'\r\n---\r\n\r\n# Host portability rules\r\n\r\nPublished source runs on Windows and Linux. Write one implementation that is correct on each host,\r\nor gate the host-specific branch on the host that needs it.\r\n\r\nTake the test half from `.claude/rules/tests.md`: it governs a host-varying property inside a test\r\nand the form of a conditional skip.\r\n\r\n## Host branching\r\n\r\n- Branch on a capability a runtime probe answers - a returned value, a thrown code, a present\r\n  member - wherever the difference is detectable.\r\n- Branch on `process.platform` only where the behavior is platform-defined, and name that fact in\r\n  the symbol's `@remarks`.\r\n- Put a platform-literal table - install root, executable suffix, toolchain name - inside a\r\n  `process.platform` branch, and read it on that platform alone.\r\n- Never flatten a correctly-gated host branch into a single path, and never relax a fail-closed\r\n  check to make one host pass.\r\n\r\n## Line endings\r\n\r\n- Split arrived text on `/\\r\\n|\\n/`. Never split on `\\n` alone, and never split on a bare `\\r`.\r\n- Split first, then trim each line; trimming the whole payload leaves a `\\r` on every line but the\r\n  last.\r\n- Frame a named protocol - HTTP, LSP, RFC 6455, NDJSON - with the terminator that protocol fixes,\r\n  and leave that terminator alone.\r\n- Emit `\\n` from code this package owns. Never emit `os.EOL`.\r\n\r\n## Paths\r\n\r\n- Normalize each operand of a path comparison with the same normalizer before comparing.\r\n- Never case-fold a containment check. Fold a lookup key only by the file-name case sensitivity the\r\n  consumer declares, and fold the whole key when you fold at all.\r\n- Compose, split, and relativize a host path with `node:path`. Never concatenate a separator\r\n  literal.\r\n- Keep a storage key, an archive key, and a URI path slash-separated, and refuse `\\` and a drive\r\n  prefix inside one.\r\n- Refuse the Windows reserved device names and the filename characters Windows rejects, on every\r\n  host.\r\n- Read the temporary directory from `os.tmpdir()`. Never write a `/tmp` literal in source.\r\n- Build a `file:` URI with `pathToFileURL`. Never format one from a path string.\r\n- Convert a `file:` URI to a host path with `fileURLToPath`. Never read `URL.pathname` as\r\n  a host path or strip the scheme, decode escapes, or rewrite a drive prefix by hand.\r\n\r\n## Processes and executables\r\n\r\n- Resolve, spawn, and terminate a child through `@orkestrel/process` where the package declares it.\r\n- Where it is not declared, spawn `process.execPath` with a JavaScript entry. Never spawn a `.bin`\r\n  shim, and never add `shell: true` to reach one.\r\n- Take a resolver's first match only after splitting its output into lines and trimming each one.\r\n  `where` prints a match per line and can name a file that is not an executable.\r\n- Treat `fs.constants.X_OK` as an existence check on Windows, where `accessSync` passes on a plain\r\n  file. Confirm an executable by its name and its file type instead.\r\n- Read an `fs.constants` member before putting it in a flag word. Windows leaves `O_NOFOLLOW`\r\n  undefined, so `O_WRONLY | O_NOFOLLOW` refuses nothing there. Never polyfill the missing member:\r\n  compensate with a check the host supports, or record the limit.\r\n- Read `PROGRAMFILES`, `LOCALAPPDATA`, and `APPDATA` inside the win32 branch and `HOME` inside the\r\n  POSIX branch. Never read one host's root on the other.\r\n- Terminate a Windows child by its process tree. A `SIGTERM` handler never runs there, so gate every\r\n  cooperative-shutdown path on POSIX.\r\n- Treat a permission bit as advisory on Windows: a written mode does not round-trip\r\n  (`chmodSync(dir, 0o500)` reads back `0444` there), so assert modes only where a probe reports they\r\n  round-trip.\r\n- Retry an `EBUSY`, `ENOTEMPTY`, or `EPERM` removal inside a bounded budget, and report the failure\r\n  when the budget ends.\r\n\r\n## Terminals\r\n\r\n- Gate styling and interactivity on `isTTY`, `NO_COLOR`, and `FORCE_COLOR`, never on the platform.\r\n- Decode `\\r`, `\\n`, and `\\r\\n` from a key stream as the same key.\r\n- Never call a Windows console-mode API. Node exposes none, so record the unreached behavior as a\r\n  limit.\r\n\r\n## Scripts and packaging\r\n\r\n- Write every `package.json` script as a portable command: a Node invocation or an installed binary.\r\n  Never name a `.sh` file there.\r\n- Keep `#!/usr/bin/env node` on an npm bin. npm writes the Windows shim from it.\r\n- Keep an agent hook under `scripts/` in bash, and keep `.gitattributes` `eol=lf`.\r\n\r\n## Claims\r\n\r\n- Never describe the suite, the host, or CI as POSIX. Name the host and the date behind a reading.\r\n- Run the gate chain on each host a change claims, and record a reading that could not run as a\r\n  limit naming the host it needs.\r\n- Keep a POSIX-shaped `file:///` literal in a guide only as an opaque fictional identifier. Build\r\n  any example a reader executes with `pathToFileURL`.\r\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

Each\r\ncross-cutting project has its own script too: `test:policy`, `test:config`, `test:setup`, `test:guides`,\r\n`test:conformance`, `test:distribution`, `test:integration`, `test:service`.\r\n\r\n## Typechecking and environment isolation\r\n\r\n`npm run check` is the comprehensive contract. It typechecks the whole tree and\r\nthen runs the configured scoped checks that prove environment isolation.\r\n\r\n- Use plain `tsc` for a TypeScript-only tree.\r\n- Use `vue-tsc` only for a scope containing `.vue` internals.\r\n- `check:src` mirrors configured `src:*` test projects; optional `src:bin` is its\r\n  own scope.\r\n- `check:app` mirrors configured `app:*` projects.\r\n- During development, run the narrowest granular scope that covers the change.\r\n- Lint is a separate complementary gate; neither lint nor root checking replaces\r\n  environment-isolation checks.\r\n\r\n| Scope                        | `lib`                             | `types`           | Permitted host globals                                                                                                                   |\r\n| ---------------------------- | --------------------------------- | ----------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\r\n| `src:core`, `app:core`       | `[\"ESNext\",\"WebWorker\"]`          | `[]`              | WHATWG web interop: fetch family, streams, URL, Abort, encoders, crypto, timers, console, DOMException, structuredClone; no DOM, no Node |\r\n| `src:browser`, `app:browser` | `[\"ESNext\",\"DOM\",\"DOM.Iterable\"]` | default           | DOM; no Node                                                                                                                             |\r\n| `src:server`, `app:server`   | `[\"ESNext\"]`                      | `[\"node\"]`        | Node; no DOM                                                                                                                             |\r\n| `src:styles`                 | `[\"ESNext\"]`                      | `[\"vite/client\"]` | Vite SCSS module declaration only                                                                                                        |\r\n\r\nStrict core is load-bearing:\r\n\r\n- Put a host-dependent helper in its host environment. A `generateId` reading `node:crypto` belongs in server, not core.\r\n- Core declares `WebWorker` to widen the WHATWG interop surface, not to admit a worker host. Policy fences these worker-only globals out of core sources: `name`, `onrtctransform`, `close`, `postMessage`, `dispatchEvent`, `location`, `onerror`, `onlanguagechange`, `onoffline`, `ononline`, `onrejectionhandled`, `onunhandledrejection`, `self`, `importScripts`, `fonts`, `caches`, `crossOriginIsolated`, `indexedDB`, `isSecureContext`, `origin`, `scheduler`, `createImageBitmap`, `reportError`, `cancelAnimationFrame`, `requestAnimationFrame`, `onmessage`, `onmessageerror`, `addEventListener`, `removeEventListener`.\r\n\r\nBuild/check config alignment:\r\n\r\n- `configs/src/tsconfig.{core,browser,server}.json` serves emit and scoped checking.\r\n- `configs/src/tsconfig.styles.json` is check-only.\r\n- `configs/app/tsconfig.core.json` is check-only.\r\n- `configs/app/tsconfig.{browser,server}.json` is check-only.\r\n- Root `tsconfig.json` keeps all libs/types for IDE and comprehensive checking; scoped configs tighten each environment.\r\n\r\n## Script intent\r\n\r\n| Script                  | Contract                                                                   |\r\n| ----------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\r\n| `dev`                   | Browser development entry                                                  |\r\n| `build`                 | Build configured library/application targets                               |\r\n| `serve` / `serve:build` | Run built server / build then run                                          |\r\n| `showcase`              | Showcase dev server                                                        |\r\n| `build:showcase`        | Build `dist/showcase`                                                      |\r\n| `show`                  | Build and copy showcase to `demo/showcase.html`                            |\r\n| `lint`                  | `oxlint --config .oxlintrc.json --fix .`; separate from typecheck          |\r\n| `lint:check`            | Non-mutating whole-tree lint gate                                          |\r\n| `check`                 | Comprehensive root typecheck plus configured isolation scopes              |\r\n| `check:<scope>`         | On-demand environment-isolation pass                                       |\r\n| `format`                | Format all files                                                           |\r\n| `format:check`          | Non-mutating whole-tree format gate                                        |\r\n| `test`                  | Environment projects plus non-isolated cross-cutting proofs                |\r\n| `clean`                 | Remove `dist/`                                                             |\r\n| `copy <from> <to>`      | Copy while creating parent directories                                     |\r\n| `prepublishOnly`        | Publishing workspaces only: the gate chain, then isolated proofs           |\r\n| `prepack`               | Publishing workspaces only: rebuild `dist/` so a pack ships current output |\r\n\r\nRun `show` only **after** formatting. The committed `demo/showcase.html` is generated/minified; formatting after generation would expand its inlined bundle.\r\n\r\n## Tooling\r\n\r\n- Typechecker: `vue-tsc` only where Vue SFCs are checked; `tsc` elsewhere.\r\n- Linter: Oxlint with `.oxlintrc.json`, independent from typechecking.\r\n- Formatter: Oxfmt with `.oxfmtrc.json`.\r\n- Bundler: Vite.\r\n- Tests: Vitest; `@vitest/browser-playwright` for browser projects.\r\n- Node build targets derive from the package's declared supported runtime. Keep `engines`, bundler targets, scoped configs, tests, and documentation aligned; never hard-code one Node version line-wide.\r\n- Browser framework: Vue 3 when present.\r\n\r\nPolicy instruments:\r\n\r\n- Put every rule of the policy law in exactly one instrument. `configs/policy.ts` - the\r\n  oxlint plugin, namespace `policy` - takes the rules a single file's AST decides. The policy sweep\r\n  (`tests/setupPolicy.ts`) takes the rules that are path- or text-shaped, and every rule whose\r\n  subject is suppression itself.\r\n- Choose between them by what can defeat the rule: an instrument must not be suppressible by the\r\n  thing it polices. A file-level `oxlint-disable` silently defeats every lint rule in its file,\r\n  plugin rules included, and nothing inside a file can suppress the sweep.\r\n- Write each visitor in the plugin's visitor table as a one-line context-binding arrow delegating to\r\n  a named module-scope `report{Noun}` function. Never write rule logic inline in the table. That\r\n  arrow is the sanctioned exception to the in-body function-expression limits in\r\n  `.claude/rules/architecture.md` for exactly that table.\r\n- Name no individual rule id here. This section fixes the instruments and how work is assigned\r\n  between them; each rule's substance stays with the law it enforces.\r\n\r\n## Text integrity\r\n\r\n- Store text as UTF-8.\r\n- Before accepting broad generated or migrated edits, scan changed text for replacement characters, mojibake, unintended control characters, and accidental trailing debris.\r\n- Preserve intentional Unicode punctuation and symbols; do not \"clean\" valid text merely because it is non-ASCII.\r\n- Never renormalize Unicode while rewriting a file. Retyping a line can silently fold a decomposed sequence into its precomposed form - `e` + U+0301 becoming U+00E9 - and the two render identically, so the diff reads as a no-op and review sees nothing. Where the exact code points are the subject, as in an encoding or transport proof, that fold deletes the case the test exists for while leaving it green and named. Move such a line rather than retyping it, and compare bytes with `od -c` or a code-point dump rather than by eye.\r\n---\r\npaths:\r\n  - 'src/**/*.ts'\r\n  - 'app/**/*.{ts,vue}'\r\n  - 'tests/**/*.ts'\r\n  - 'configs/**/*.ts'\r\n  - 'vite.config.ts'\r\n  - 'tsconfig.json'\r\n  - 'package.json'\r\n---\r\n\r\n# Architecture and placement rules\r\n\r\n## Centralized-file pattern\r\n\r\n| Content                   | Sole location                                                |\r\n| ------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ |\r\n| Interfaces/types          | `*/types.ts`                                                 |\r\n| Constants/data            | `*/constants.ts`                                             |\r\n| Template definitions      | `*/templates.ts`                                             |\r\n| Pure helpers              | `*/helpers.ts`                                               |\r\n| Guards                    | `*/validators.ts`                                            |\r\n| Guard combinators         | `*/combinators.ts`                                           |\r\n| Owned snapshots           | `*/cloners.ts`                                               |\r\n| Coercers                  | `*/parsers.ts`                                               |\r\n| Shape values              | `*/shapers.ts`                                               |\r\n| Value inferers            | `*/inferers.ts`                                              |\r\n| Shape/algorithm compilers | `*/compilers.ts`                                             |\r\n| Entity/value factories    | `*/factories.ts`                                             |\r\n| Middleware factories      | `*/middlewares.ts`                                           |\r\n| Request handlers          | `*/handlers.ts`                                              |\r\n| Route tables              | `*/routes.ts`                                                |\r\n| Seeders                   | `*/seeders.ts`                                               |\r\n| Schemas                   | `*/schemas.ts`                                               |\r\n| Compiled contracts        | `*/contracts.ts`                                             |\r\n| Relations                 | `*/relations.ts`                                             |\r\n| Error classes/guards      | `*/errors.ts`                                                |\r\n| Public exports            | `*/index.ts`                                                 |\r\n| Implementations           | `*/[domain]/[Entity].ts` - one class per file                |\r\n| Function modules          | a designated folder's `[function].ts`, one function per file |\r\n\r\nUse only the centralized files an environment needs.\r\n\r\n## Declaration placement\r\n\r\n- An implementation file contains imports and exactly one class implementation with `#` fields.\r\n- It contains no module-scope interface, type, constant, or free function-even when private to that file.\r\n- A function module contains imports and exactly one exported function named for its file. Everything else extracts by kind exactly as it does from a class module.\r\n- Extract local declarations by kind. \"Only used here\" and \"not exported\" are not exemptions.\r\n- Every declaration in a centralized file is exported. Fold away a trivial single-use declaration or export/test it; never leave it hidden.\r\n- The only permitted non-exported module-scope declarations are in a runtime entrypoint that must be self-contained and cannot import siblings, such as raw source loaded in a worker. Explain that necessity in a comment.\r\n- A runtime entry-`src/bin/main.ts`, `app/browser/main.ts`, `app/server/main.ts`-is a fixed name, not a centralized kind file. Both the data rule and the function rule reach it, so it declares no module-scope constant and no module-scope function: it imports what it needs and runs. The preceding self-contained exception covers only an entrypoint that cannot import siblings.\r\n- Perform a cleanup pass after implementation: no stray implementation-file declarations, non-exported/wrong-kind centralized declarations, prohibited nested declarations, duplicate implementations, compatibility aliases, superfluous wrappers, stale imports/barrel rows, or untested extracted functions.\r\n\r\n## Kind purity\r\n\r\n- Each centralized file contains only its named kind.\r\n- Module-scope constants live only in `constants.ts`, use UPPER_SNAKE_CASE, and freeze object/array data with `Object.freeze`.\r\n- A camelCase namespace containing functions is helper behavior, not constant data; place it in `helpers.ts`.\r\n- `helpers.ts` is exported reusable infrastructure. A reusable regex, parser, header flattener, signer, or similar fragment has one implementation used by source, tests, and fixtures.\r\n- A would-be helper has these outcomes:\r\n  - trivial and genuinely single-use \u001a fold into its caller;\r\n  - non-trivial or reusable \u001a extract, export, unit-test, and route every duplicate through it.\r\n- `factories.ts`, `compilers.ts`, and `parsers.ts` are centralized files, not hiding places. Factory glue extracts to `helpers.ts`; pure compiler/parser recursion remains exported in its own kind file.\r\n- Every exported function in `parsers.ts` is named `parse*`. Every exported function in\r\n  `factories.ts` is named `create*`.\r\n- Those name forms are one-directional. A name does not place a function: `createWriteDirectory`\r\n  creates a directory rather than an entity and `isVacant` is a predicate rather than a `Guard<T>`,\r\n  so both stay in `helpers.ts`. Placement follows what the function is; the name form follows\r\n  placement.\r\n- Repair a violation of those forms by deciding what the function **is** first, then moving or\r\n  renaming to match. Both repairs exist and picking the wrong one does real damage.\r\n  - Wrong file, right name \u001a **move it**. A `scan*` in `parsers.ts` is a pure lexical leaf that\r\n    belongs in `helpers.ts`. The barrel star-exports both, so the move leaves the published surface\r\n    identical.\r\n  - Right file, wrong name \u001a **rename it in place**. An entity is a class instance whose methods\r\n    drive its own state, as opposed to a plain value that carries data and no behaviour. A function\r\n    returning a live entity is an entity factory and belongs in `factories.ts` whatever it is\r\n    called, so `restoreThing` there is misnamed, not misplaced. Renaming moves the published\r\n    surface and earns a version bump; that cost is the correct one to pay, and it is smaller than\r\n    the alternative.\r\n  - Never let the name choose. Relocating a correctly-placed function to escape a rename drags its\r\n    dependencies with it - an entity factory moved into `helpers.ts` makes that file import an\r\n    implementation class, and a leaf file that imports a class stops being a leaf for every module\r\n    beneath it.\r\n- Keep the leaf pair class-free. `helpers.ts` and `validators.ts` sit at the bottom of a module's\r\n  graph: they import types, constants, errors, and each other, and they import no implementation\r\n  class. Every file that constructs or drives a class - `cloners.ts`, `compilers.ts`, `factories.ts`,\r\n  `shapers.ts` - sits above them, consumes them, and is never consumed by them. One cycle between\r\n  the leaves is the shape this produces and is acceptable; an edge running downward from a\r\n  class-importing file into the leaf pair is not.\r\n- `templates.ts` and `contracts.ts` hold data only - shipped template definitions and compiled\r\n  contracts. A function that builds either belongs in the kind file for what it builds.\r\n- `handlers.ts` holds request handlers, which are functions. `routes.ts` holds data only: a route is\r\n  `{ method, path, handler }`, so the table maps each path to a handler declared in `handlers.ts`\r\n  and referenced by name, never to a function expression written in place. Neither file mixes kinds and the\r\n  route set stays readable as data.\r\n\r\n### What the policy instruments prove\r\n\r\nThe `policy` Oxlint plugin (`configs/policy.ts`) enforces syntactic placement: a declaration of a\r\ngiven syntactic kind appears only in a file permitted to hold that kind. The fleet policy sweep\r\n(`tests/policy.test.ts`, `tests/setupPolicy.ts`) enforces what a path or a text reading decides.\r\nThe plugin reads declaration syntax and file name; the sweep reads paths and workspace text;\r\nneither reads meaning.\r\n\r\n- The plugin proves that a module function sits in a function-kind file, that module data sits in a\r\n  data-kind file, that every centralized declaration is exported, that a class sits in its matching\r\n  implementation or errors file, and that `constants.ts` declares only UPPER_SNAKE_CASE consts with\r\n  no bare collection literal.\r\n- The sweep proves that no source, test, config, or script file carries an `eslint-disable` or\r\n  `oxlint-disable` directive.\r\n- The sweep proves that every `.claude/rules/*.md` file has a rule-map row in `AGENTS.md` and that\r\n  every row resolves to a file.\r\n- The sweep proves the host portability rules that are path- or text-shaped over the population\r\n  `POLICY_PORTABILITY_GLOB` names: no path segment carries a Windows reserved device name, a\r\n  character Windows refuses, a trailing dot or space, or a sibling differing only by case; and no\r\n  `package.json` script names a `.sh` file.\r\n- The plugin proves, over the population `POLICY_ENDING_GLOBS` names, that no source trims a\r\n  payload before splitting it on `'\\n'`, reads `os.EOL`, or imports `EOL` from `node:os`.\r\n- The sweep cannot write a filename the host refuses. A Windows host rejects `<`, folds a case\r\n  collision into one file, and turns `:` into an alternate data stream, so those boundaries are\r\n  proven from a path population rather than from written files.\r\n- The plugin does not prove a collection is frozen. It reads the declaration, never the value a\r\n  call returns, so `Object.freeze([.])` and any other call initializer are one syntax to it. The\r\n  freeze obligation in the earlier kind-purity rules binds regardless; only the bare literal is\r\n  mechanical.\r\n- The plugin does not tell one function kind from another. Every centralized file that permits\r\n  functions reads the same to it apart from the `parse*` and `create*` name forms: `cloners.ts`,\r\n  `combinators.ts`, `compilers.ts`, `errors.ts`, `factories.ts`, `handlers.ts`, `helpers.ts`,\r\n  `inferers.ts`, `middlewares.ts`, `parsers.ts`, `relations.ts`, `schemas.ts`, `seeders.ts`,\r\n  `shapers.ts`, and `validators.ts`. That list is exhaustive, a new function kind joins it, and no\r\n  later version of the plugin claims more.\r\n- The plugin reports no `data` violation in `helpers.ts`. The kind rules place a camelCase\r\n  namespace of functions there, and a namespace of callables is not separable from a data table by\r\n  declaration syntax, so `DATA_EXEMPT_FILES` in `configs/policy.ts` excludes the file. Ordinary\r\n  module data there - `export const RETRIES = 3` - is unreported; the earlier constants rule binds\r\n  regardless.\r\n- No placement or line-ending rule inspects an ambient declaration file: `.d.ts`, `.d.mts`, and\r\n  `.d.cts` are outside their reach. The lint population reaches those files, and each of those\r\n  rules refuses the file by its name, because an ambient declaration file is not a module in the\r\n  kind table.\r\n- The plugin does not inspect class-expression members. A function assigned inside a\r\n  class-expression method is unreported; the earlier functions rule still binds, and cleanup and\r\n  review enforce it.\r\n- The cleanup pass and independent review prove kind purity across those files. A helper misfiled\r\n  as a parser, a coercer misfiled as a guard, a compiler misfiled as a factory, and a shaper\r\n  misfiled as a cloner are review findings, not instrument diagnostics.\r\n- The plugin does not decide barrel membership. It parses each file alone and resolves no module,\r\n  so it cannot tell whether a declaration is reachable from its barrel. That question belongs to\r\n  each package's `tests/guides.test.ts`, which imports the barrel and gets real resolution. Do not\r\n  add module resolution here to duplicate it.\r\n- The kind table is mandatory whether or not an instrument can see the violation.\r\n\r\n## Wrapper test\r\n\r\nA wrapper survives only when it adds a real boundary, invariant, composition, translation, lifecycle, or materially narrower contract.\r\n\r\n- Delete one-line delegates, pass-through factories, rename-only helpers/getters, compatibility aliases, and wrappers around semantically identical platform or declared-dependency primitives.\r\n- Rename or import the real symbol and update every consumer atomically. Do not preserve a wrapper to avoid downstream edits.\r\n- Do not re-export a dependency's symbol from this package.\r\n- A public class method composes real entity behavior; it never exists only to forward 1:1 to a helper.\r\n- Audit callers and callees after every extraction so the old path and duplicate implementation do not remain.\r\n\r\n## Functions and orchestration\r\n\r\n- Never declare or assign a function inside another function or method.\r\n- This bans local `function`, `function*`, and `const fn = () => ...`, regardless of caller count.\r\n- The only in-body function expressions allowed are an anonymous callback passed directly as an argument and an anonymous function returned directly as the result (the factory/combinator pattern).\r\n- Instance-bound work that reaches state or sibling methods is a method, not a free function.\r\n\r\nSeparate these roles:\r\n\r\n1. **Public method:** implements the interface and genuinely composes behavior; never forwards 1:1 to one helper.\r\n2. **`#` private method:** stateful/instance-bound orchestration or the class's defining recursive/compositional algorithm.\r\n3. **Centralized helper:** pure referentially transparent leaf, independently understandable/testable without class knowledge.\r\n\r\nApply the leaf test:\r\n\r\n1. Reaches `#` state or a sibling method \u001a private method.\r\n2. Pure self-contained computation (key, format, compare, convert, lookup, projection, one unification) \u001a exported helper.\r\n3. Recursive spine or composition of leaves (`solve`, `isolate`, `prove`, chaining, relational join), even if pure \u001a private method.\r\n4. Defining engine internals remain methods when extraction would hollow the class into a thin delegate.\r\n\r\nExtract pure leaves aggressively; keep the class's actual behavior.\r\n\r\n## Class order\r\n\r\n1. `#` private fields: context, options, state/result, child managers.\r\n2. Constructor: initialize context/options and instantiate child managers.\r\n3. Public interface: getters, then methods.\r\n4. `#` private methods.\r\n\r\nStore child managers in `#` fields and expose readonly getters typed as their interfaces.\r\n\r\n## Middleware\r\n\r\n- Middleware is behavior `(context, next) => ...`, never a class with one `handle` method.\r\n- Place middleware factories in `middlewares.ts` as `createX(options): Middleware`; keep them distinct from entity/value factories.\r\n- Keep middleware-local state closure-private.\r\n- If consumers must address, share, inspect, or replace that state, extract the state to a pluggable class supplied as an option, with an in-memory default.\r\n- Extract reusable cross-middleware machinery to helpers/classes.\r\n- `MiddlewareManager` is the sole manager of the middleware composition chain; do not create one manager per middleware.\r\n\r\n## Environment/module placement\r\n\r\n- Shared cross-environment logic belongs in the central core/shared layer. Other environments import core; core imports neither browser nor server.\r\n- An environment with multiple modules keeps cross-module types/helpers at the environment root and module-specific declarations inside the module.\r\n- Promote a declaration to the environment root only when at least two modules use it.\r\n- The environment barrel re-exports root files and module barrels.\r\n- Cover cross-cutting environment-root helpers with behavioral tests; each module API receives its own guide parity.\r\n\r\n### Entity subfolders\r\n\r\n- When one entity grows a family (entity + manager or sibling implementations), nest only its class files in a lowercase plural folder.\r\n- Keep its interfaces in module-root `types.ts`, factories in module-root `factories.ts`, and exports in the module barrel.\r\n- Entity subfolders never grow their own centralized files.\r\n\r\n### Kind or folder\r\n\r\n- A word is either a centralized kind or a domain folder, never both.\r\n- A folder named for a centralized kind-`helpers/`, `validators/`, `handlers/`-is that kind's file, not a folder.\r\n- `FUNCTION_DOMAIN_FOLDERS` in the fleet-canon register (`configs/policy.ts`) registers the\r\n  folder paths whose direct modules are function modules. Registration makes a path eligible and\r\n  fixes the declaration shape checked there; it judges nothing about what a module does.\r\n- Never infer a function domain from a folder's name; a camelCase module inside an unregistered\r\n  folder is misplaced.\r\n- Register a folder only when its direct modules are camelCase single-function `.ts` modules. A\r\n  folder of PascalCase single-file components, or of entity classes, never qualifies.\r\n- Request a new domain through a fleet-canon change. There is no workspace-local registration path.\r\n\r\n### Extension categories\r\n\r\n- A designed extension point-drivers, stores, transports-gets its category folder even with one concrete class.\r\n- This applies both to consumer implementations of another domain's contract and default implementations beside the defining contract.\r\n- The contract remains in module-root `types.ts`; only concrete classes nest.\r\n- Nest because the category is a designed growth seam, not because a class name happens to end in `Store` or `Driver`.\r\n- Co-equal native-object wrappers that are core primitives stay flat.\r\n\r\n### Stores\r\n\r\nChoose the access shape:\r\n\r\n- **Point access:** `get`, `set`, `delete`.\r\n- **Bulk restore:** `save`, `load`, `remove`, `clear`.\r\n\r\nEvery shape obeys:\r\n\r\n- The stored value carries its own id; do not pass a separate id to `set`/`save`.\r\n- Every primitive is async and returns a `Promise`.\r\n- Deleting/removing a missing key is a no-op.\r\n- Concrete stores live in `stores/`; the interface stays in module-root `types.ts`; `create*Store` factories stay in `factories.ts`.\r\n\r\n## Barrel exports\r\n\r\n- `*/index.ts` is the sole public barrel.\r\n- Only an `index.ts` may re-export.\r\n- A barrel contains only `export * from './module.js'` declarations. Do not use\r\n  named, default, namespace, or type-only barrel exports.\r\n- A star-export collision is a design failure: rename the conflicting concept\r\n  at its owner and update consumers. Never hide the collision with a selective\r\n  barrel row.\r\n- Never re-export a symbol originating in another package; fix consumer imports to the originating package.\r\n- Implementation files export their own classes directly.\r\n- Expose every intentional top-level source export through its correct environment barrel.\r\n- Never let current consumer count gate later exposure. Developers receive the same supported\r\n  mechanisms the package uses, so they retain full control and customization.\r\n- If a declaration must not be public, make it a true local or runtime-private detail, or remove\r\n  the capability for a substantive reason. Never leave an intentional reusable export stranded\r\n  outside the barrel.\r\n- One-class-per-file evicts some classes from their only caller, and `export` on such a file is\r\n  structural rather than a statement of intent. Barrel that class when a consumer can construct it\r\n  from values they already hold. Intern it - out of the barrel, and named in the package's parity\r\n  `INTERNAL` list - when its constructor requires a value only its owner produces, or when the\r\n  public value is a projection of the instance rather than the instance. A class named in a public\r\n  signature is always barrelled.\r\n- Delete a barrel row whose class no consumer can construct, and delete its `@example` with it. A\r\n  row obliges a documented, runnable example, so a class kept public without one is drift that\r\n  parity cannot see.\r\n- When a symbol moves, update every import; never leave a compatibility re-export.\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nexport * from './types.js'\r\nexport * from './constants.js'\r\nexport * from './errors.js'\r\nexport * from './validators.js'\r\nexport * from './helpers.js'\r\nexport * from './factories.js'\r\nexport * from './greeters/Greeter.js'\r\n```\r\n\r\n## System constraints\r\n\r\n- Build or substantively expand a capability with its first real consumer; do not speculate. This\r\n  gate applies only when creating or expanding the capability, not to its later barrel exposure.\r\n- Keep interfaces to the smallest primitives the capability requires.\r\n- For multiple backends, implement shared querying/paging/aggregation in one engine over those primitives. A backend may override an operation only with a genuine native fast path and otherwise falls back to the engine.\r\n- Centralize any pattern repeated twice.\r\n- Keep everything generic/reusable and free of unrelated-project logic.\r\n- Do not expand the capability set without concrete need. Once that capability exists intentionally,\r\n  its reusable top-level exports follow the earlier barrel rule without a second consumer gate.\r\n- Do not remove structural files because they are empty.\r\n- Prefer the smallest complete implementation that preserves architecture.\r\n- No deprecation aliases, compatibility shims, or backward-compatibility branches; update all consumers atomically.\r\n- No polling/busy loops or recursive microtasks as architecture. Park idle work on an event/abort wakeup and yield long work in cooperative quanta.\r\n- Framework/server utilities provide mechanism-HMAC, CORS, parsing, sessions, cookies, CSRF, static serving-not product policy such as user models, login flow, or authorization.\r\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

Tool and workspace methods live in their dependency guides.\n738:#### `AgentProviderInterface`\n740:The seams a subclass fills, beneath the boundary members it inherits. `AgentProviderInterface` extends `ProviderInterface`, so `generate` and `stream` are part of it and repeat here with their boundary contracts; `AgentProvider` implements each of them once, for every subclass. A subclass writes `frame` / `body` / `read` / `finish` and nothing else. The `id` / `name` / `format` data members stay Surface rows.\n760:#### `RelayProvider`\n971:## Contract\n977:3. **`stream` yields deltas + returns the assembled result.** A `ProviderInterface.stream` yields each non-empty answer delta as `{ channel: 'content', text }` and each native live reasoning delta as `{ channel: 'thinking', text }`; its return value is the assembled `ProviderResult` whose `content` is the provider's authoritative clean answer, plus any tool calls and usage the turn reported. So a caller can render tokens and reasoning live while still recovering the complete outcome from the generator's return value. A thinking model's reasoning is separated once, at the wire that carried it. A provider reading a raw wire routes its content through a `ThinkSplitter` (`createThinkSplitter` — one per stream, armed by the base's `split` switch), yields the clean content, assembles `content` from the splitter's authoritative accumulation (an implicit pre-seeded open — the qwen3-template bare `</think>` — reclassifies the already-yielded prefix into thinking, the one shape where live content deltas can transiently over-report), and surfaces the accumulated reasoning as `ProviderResult.thinking` — which never re-enters the conversation (the `Agent` joins it across a run's calls onto `AgentResult.thinking` as display/audit metadata). A relay's deltas arrive already separated by the upstream provider that did that work, so `RelayProvider` constructs with `split: false` and preserves each delta verbatim, a literal `<think>` tag in the answer included: splitting a second time would re-classify text the upstream already ruled on (the relay-protocol clause).\n980:6. **A local cancel and a remotely reported one are different failures.** A `stream` cancelled mid-flight throws a `ProviderAbortError` whose `partial` is the `ProviderResult` assembled from whatever streamed so far (content + any tool calls + any usage); `isProviderAbortError` narrows a caught value so the loop can recover the partial content. A non-abort error propagates unchanged. `ProviderAbortError` is the boundary's error — it stays in this module so the agent loop catches it regardless of which backend is in use. The origins stay distinguishable. A **local** cancel is the combined bound firing: the engine assembles the partial from what it decoded locally, and when a throw raced that cancel it rides along as the error's `cause` rather than replacing it. A **remote** abort is a report, not a cancel: a relay `abort` frame makes `RelayProvider.read` throw a reconstructed `ProviderAbortError` carrying the upstream's own partial while the local signal stays unaborted, so the engine propagates that instance unchanged instead of folding it into a local partial, and the `Agent` runtime treats it as a genuine error unless its own bound signal aborted. A caller that cancels locally therefore always outranks the wire: the local partial is what it gets.\n1014:34. **The provider engine and its seams (`AgentProvider`).** The instance owns a minted `id` (`crypto.randomUUID()`), taken in the constructor and returned by every call it serves, and `format` exposed exactly as supplied. The base owns, for each call: the deadline folded with the caller's signal (the bounding clause); `AgentProviderInput.fetch`, or the global transport bound to its global receiver; an awaited `headers` hook merged over a `Content-Type: application/json` default and raced against that combined bound, so an unresolved hook cannot outlive the call and its abort listener is released on every exit; one `POST` of `JSON.stringify(body(request))` to `url + (path ?? '')`; the response body decoded through `readChunks`; one `frame()` parser armed for that call; every framed record through `read`; the `finish(parser)` tail fed through `read` as well; the splitter flushed as a final content delta; and the outcome assembled by `buildProviderResult`. A subclass owns `name`, `frame`, `body`, `read`, and `finish`, and nothing else — an implementation that needs a second transport, a second deadline, or a second error taxonomy has left the seam rather than extended it. `split` (default `true`) arms one `ThinkSplitter` for the call; `strict` (default `false`) decides how a stream may end — with `strict: true` a stream that reaches end of input carrying no `ProviderIncrement.result` throws `ProviderError('PROTOCOL', 'provider error: missing settled result')`, and with `strict: false` the engine assembles the result from its accumulation instead. A record whose `read` returns a `result` is authoritative: the engine returns it at once without folding that record's other fields, leaves any later record undecoded, and cancels the body. **The bounded error read.** A non-OK response's body is decoded through `readText` to at most `MAX_ERROR_BODY_LENGTH` bytes and its remainder is cancelled, so a stalled error body is refused inside the deadline rather than after it. The bound counts bytes handed to the decoder. A source may deliver one chunk larger than the remaining budget; the read admits that chunk's leading bytes up to the budget and cancels the remainder, so the excerpt is decoded from at most `MAX_ERROR_BODY_LENGTH` source bytes and the read may have pulled one whole source chunk from the network. A multibyte character cut at that bound decodes to a replacement character, so the excerpt's own encoded length can exceed the bound by that character. **Reader-owned cancellation.** `readText` and `readChunks` each own their reader: each registers its abort listener on its own controller, cancels the source when the bound fires, and releases the lock in a `finally`, so a cancellation that itself fails never escapes and a body is never left locked. The parser clear and the deadline clear sit in nested `finally` blocks, so one failing cleanup never skips the other.\n1016:35. **The wire shapes and the compiled contracts.** `toolCallShape`, `messageShape`, `providerRequestShape`, `providerResultShape`, and `relayFrameShape` declare each domain type's JSON projection, and `messageContract`, `providerRequestContract`, `providerResultContract`, and `relayFrameContract` compile them into guards and parsers. Every projection is strictly narrower than the domain type it mirrors, and the narrowing is the point: `ToolCall.arguments` is `Record<string, unknown>` in the domain and a JSON record on the wire, so a function-valued argument is refused rather than dropped silently, and a tool's `parameters` and a call's `schema` are refused the same way. `ToolCall.caller` is local context and appears in no shape — the guard refuses a record carrying it and the parser drops it, so it cannot cross a hop. `relayFrameShape` is a channel-discriminated union that admits no member outside the arm it matched, so a decorative field on an `error` frame is a refusal rather than a tolerated extra. The round trip is closed in both directions: the request `RelayProvider.body` projects parses back to the value the relay hands upstream, and the frame `RelayStream` writes parses back to the delta or the result the browser decodes. **The wire body is an owned snapshot.** `body` clones the projection through Contract's `cloneJSONValue` and validates that clone, so the bytes `JSON.stringify` produces are exactly what the guard saw. The clone reads property descriptors rather than accessors, so a serializer reachable only through a `get` trap or a prototype is never consulted; an own function-valued property on a call's `arguments`, a tool's `parameters`, or the options `schema` — such as a `toJSON` method — is a value outside JSON and is refused before fetching, with the clone's own failure as the refusal's `cause` — the snapshot is the sole mechanism, and the refusal that remains also covers a hostile read and a snapshot the contract rejects. A caller mutating its own objects after the call cannot change what was sent.\n1018:36. **The relay protocol (`createRelay` / `RelayStream` / `RelayProvider`).** The request is a `POST` whose body is one `providerRequestContract` JSON document — `{ messages, tools?, options? }` and nothing beyond it. The response is newline-delimited `RelayFrame` records under `RELAY_CONTENT_TYPE` with `cache-control: no-store`, written under response backpressure: `RelayStream` awaits one `provider.stream` step per pull, so a slow reader parks the upstream turn rather than buffering it. `RelayProvider` constructs on `split: false` and `strict: true`, so the browser end preserves each delta verbatim and treats a stream that ended without a `result` frame as a protocol failure rather than a silently assembled answer. **The frame vocabulary.** `{ channel: 'content' | 'thinking', text }` carries each delta, `{ channel: 'result', result }` carries the settled turn, `{ channel: 'abort', partial }` carries an upstream cancel with its partial, and `{ channel: 'error', message }` carries every other failure — `message` is always `RELAY_PROVIDER_MESSAGE`, so an upstream failure's own text never reaches the browser. **`authorize` and its obligations.** It is mandatory, it runs before the body is read, and it must not consume that body: a body-reading hook locks the stream and the handler answers `400`. The mechanism performs no origin check and no method check, so an application authorizing on an ambient credential such as a cookie must compose origin and CSRF middleware in front of the handler; a bearer header the browser sets explicitly is not reachable cross-site and needs no such composition. **The refusals**, each carrying no body: `401` when `authorize` returns anything but `true` or throws; `413` when the request body reaches `limit` bytes (`DEFAULT_RELAY_LIMIT` when omitted) or the inbound read is aborted — at the limit, not merely above it, because a body that fills the budget without reporting end of input is indistinguishable from one that exceeds it; `400` when the body is missing, unreadable, or rejected by the contract; `502` when the upstream provider call cannot be constructed. Each refusal reaches the browser through the engine's HTTP path as a `ProviderError` with code `'HTTP'`, that `status`, and the message `provider error: <status>` — no excerpt, because a refusal carries no body. **Cancellation runs both ways.** An inbound abort — the client disconnecting, or the request's own signal firing — aborts the upstream controller, returns the generator, and releases the request listener even with a frame still queued unread; a browser-side cancel reaches the same path through its `fetch` signal, and the local cancel wins over any frame still in flight (the local-and-remote-cancel clause). What a disconnected client cannot recover is the turn itself: the upstream call is cancelled, whatever streamed but never reached the socket is gone, nothing is replayed, and no frame is held for a reconnect. A client that needs a resumable turn owns that above the relay. **The honest browser limit.** Host independence is proven here by the core scope's typecheck (no host global is in scope), by the transport defaulting to the global `fetch` bound to its global receiver, and by a recorded Chrome 148 run of the built core entry: that entry and its whole `@orkestrel` import closure load as ES modules with no console error, and a browser-side `RelayProvider` round-trips a turn, cancels mid-stream and keeps its local partial, and receives a refused bearer as a `ProviderError` with code `'HTTP'` and status `401`. It is not proven by a browser test project, because this package has none.\n1020:37. **`ProviderError` and its codes.** `ProviderError` carries a machine-readable `code` and a `status` that is present for an `HTTP` failure and `undefined` under every other code; `ProviderErrorOptions` carries that `status` and the underlying `cause`. `isProviderError` narrows a caught value so a caller can branch on the code. `'HTTP'` reports a non-OK response: the message is `provider error: <status>`, and ` - <excerpt>` is appended only when the bounded error read returned text, so an empty body leaves no separator and no trailing space; when that read fails outright the message is `provider error: <status> - (error body unavailable)` and the read's failure rides as the `cause`. `'PROTOCOL'` reports a successful response with no body, a record the concrete provider refuses (a frame outside `relayFrameContract`, a request the JSON wire cannot carry), or a `strict` stream that ended with no settled result. `'PROVIDER'` reports an upstream failure a relay carried in an `error` frame, and its message is `RELAY_PROVIDER_MESSAGE`. A cancel is in none of them: it is `ProviderAbortError` (the local-and-remote-cancel clause).\n1054:\tProviderOptions,\n1067:interface TextOptions extends ProviderOptions {\n1095:A browser must never hold a model credential. Retyping a line can silently fold a decomposed sequence into its precomposed form - `e` + U+0301 becoming U+00E9 - and the two render identically, so the diff reads as a no-op and review sees nothing. Where the exact code points are the subject, as in an encoding or transport proof, that fold deletes the case the test exists for while leaving it green and named. Move such a line rather than retyping it, and compare bytes with `od -c` or a code-point dump rather than by eye.\r\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}
